GATE 1 — Approval Gate
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Input  : promotion_request.approval_id (UUID) + status field       │
│  Check  : approval_id IS NOT NULL AND status == "approved"          │
│  Pass   : proceeds to Gate 2                                        │
│  Fail   : ModelPromotion.status → REJECTED, reason logged           │
│  Source : backend/src/promotions/gates.py → ApprovalGate.check()   │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

GATE 2 — Frequency Performance Gate
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Input  : challenger_freq_auc, champion_freq_auc                    │
│  Check  : challenger_auc >= champion_auc - 0.005 (delta tolerance)  │
│  Pass   : challenger AUC within acceptable degradation band         │
│  Fail   : REJECTED — "frequency model underperforms champion"       │
│  Source : backend/src/promotions/gates.py → FreqPerfGate.check()   │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

GATE 3 — Severity Performance Gate
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Input  : challenger_sev_r2, champion_sev_r2                        │
│  Check  : challenger_r2 >= champion_r2 - 0.01                       │
│  Pass   : R² within acceptable regression band                      │
│  Fail   : REJECTED — "severity model underperforms champion"        │
│  Source : backend/src/promotions/gates.py → SevPerfGate.check()    │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

GATE 4 — Minimum AUC Gate (Absolute Floor)
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Input  : challenger_freq_auc                                       │
│  Check  : challenger_auc >= 0.70 (hard floor, model-agnostic)       │
│  Pass   : model meets minimum discriminative ability                │
│  Fail   : REJECTED — "AUC below minimum threshold 0.70"            │
│  Source : backend/src/promotions/gates.py → MinAucGate.check()     │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

GATE 5 — Minimum R² Gate (Absolute Floor)
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Input  : challenger_sev_r2                                         │
│  Check  : challenger_r2 >= 0.30 (hard floor)                        │
│  Pass   : severity model meets minimum explanatory power            │
│  Fail   : REJECTED — "R² below minimum threshold 0.30"             │
│  Source : backend/src/promotions/gates.py → MinR2Gate.check()      │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

GATE 6 — Shadow Deployment Gate
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Input  : shadow_count (rows in shadow_predictions table for        │
│           this challenger), bypass_shadow flag (boolean)            │
│  Check  : shadow_count > 0 OR bypass_shadow == True                 │
│  Pass   : challenger has run in shadow mode OR bypass granted       │
│  Fail   : REJECTED — "no shadow deployment evidence"               │
│  Shadow records written by: ShadowDeploymentService                 │
│    .record_background() called in quote_v2.py as BackgroundTask     │
│  Source : backend/src/promotions/gates.py → ShadowGate.check()     │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

GATE 7 — Drift Gate
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Input  : psi_score (Population Stability Index), bypass_drift flag │
│  Check  : psi_score <= 0.20 OR bypass_drift == True                 │
│  PSI ref: v4_reference_distributions.json (stored alongside models) │
│  Pass   : feature distributions stable against reference            │
│  Fail   : REJECTED — "feature drift detected PSI > 0.20"           │
│  Source : backend/src/promotions/gates.py → DriftGate.check()      │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

GATES RESULT AGGREGATION
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  GovernanceGates.check_all() iterates [G1→G7] sequentially         │
│  First failure → short-circuit, remaining gates skipped             │
│  All pass → gates_passed=[g1..g7], gates_failed=[]                  │
│  Result stored in ModelPromotion.evaluation_report (JSONB)          │
│  Promotion.status → APPROVED / REJECTED                             │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

        ↓ (if APPROVED)
PHASE 5 — PROMOTION EXECUTION
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Triggered by: Phase5BPromotionEngine.execute()                     │
│  Precondition: ModelPromotion.status == APPROVED                    │
│                                                                     │
│  Step 1: Status → PROMOTING                                         │
│  Step 2: AtomicPromotion.execute()                                  │
│    ├── Save snapshot of current champion to old_champion_snapshot   │
│    │   (JSONB in ModelPromotion row)                                │
│    ├── Write new champion paths to champion_registry.json           │
│    │   via os.replace() (atomic file swap — no partial read risk)   │
│    ├── Call reload_predictor() → atomic singleton swap              │
│    │   (new models loaded; old object dereferenced)                 │
│    └── Write new_champion_snapshot to ModelPromotion row            │
│  Step 3 (success): Status → ACTIVE                                  │
│  Step 3 (failure): Status → ROLLED_BACK (auto rollback triggered)   │
│    └── RollbackHistory row created, trigger_type="auto"             │
│  Source: backend/src/promotions/engine.py                           │
│          backend/src/promotions/atomic.py                           │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

        ↓
PHASE 6 — MONITORING AND ROLLBACK
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  ACTIVE MODEL MONITORING                                            │
│    ├── Shadow deployments continue writing to shadow_predictions    │
│    ├── PSI recalculated on schedule vs reference distributions      │
│    └── _append_governance_log() records every state transition      │
│                                                                     │
│  ROLLBACK TRIGGERS (6 types in RollbackHistory.trigger_type):      │
│    ├── auto      : AtomicPromotion.execute() failure (exception)    │
│    ├── manual    : API call → Phase5BPromotionEngine.rollback()     │
│    ├── drift     : PSI exceeds 0.20 post-activation                 │
│    ├── performance: AUC/R² degradation detected in production       │
│    ├── scheduled : APScheduler periodic evaluation triggers rollback│
│    └── api       : direct API endpoint call (admin-initiated)       │
│                                                                     │
│  ROLLBACK PROCEDURE:                                                │
│    ├── Precondition: ModelPromotion.status == ACTIVE                │
│    ├── AtomicPromotion.manual_rollback()                            │
│    │   ├── Read old_champion_snapshot from ModelPromotion JSONB     │
│    │   ├── Restore champion_registry.json to previous paths         │
│    │   └── Call reload_predictor() with previous model files        │
│    ├── Status → ROLLED_BACK                                         │
│    └── RollbackHistory row: trigger_type, reason, rolled_back_by    │
│                                                                     │
│  GOVERNANCE LOG:                                                     │
│    File: models/metadata/governance_log.json                        │
│    Every event: timestamp, model_id, old_status, new_status,        │
│                 gate_results, triggered_by                          │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

── 5. COMPONENT RESPONSIBILITIES ──────────────────────────────────────

  Phase5BPromotionEngine
    → Orchestrates evaluate() and execute() lifecycle
    → Owns status machine transitions
    → Creates DB rows for ModelPromotion, RollbackHistory

  GovernanceGates / individual Gate classes
    → Each gate is stateless, receives context dict
    → Returns GateResult(passed: bool, reason: str, metadata: dict)
    → Sequential short-circuit evaluation

  AtomicPromotion
    → Handles file-system operations (champion_registry.json swap)
    → Calls reload_predictor() for in-memory model swap
    → Creates old/new snapshots for rollback safety

  ModelRegistry
    → Reads champion_registry.json for active model paths
    → load_frequency_model() / load_severity_model() called after promotion
    → rollback_champion() / activate_champion() state writers

  ShadowDeploymentService
    → record_background() called in quote_v2.py as FastAPI BackgroundTask
    → Writes shadow predictions for Gate 6 evidence
    → Compares challenger vs champion predictions without affecting users

  reload_predictor()
    → Located in backend/src/models/predictor.py
    → Atomic singleton swap: builds new CompoundActuarialPredictor
    → Thread-safe replacement of global _predictor instance

── 6. INTERACTION BETWEEN COMPONENTS ──────────────────────────────────

  API Layer
    → POST /api/v2/quote triggers ShadowDeploymentService.record_background()
    → Admin API triggers Phase5BPromotionEngine.evaluate() / execute()

  Phase5BPromotionEngine ←→ GovernanceGates
    → Engine passes evaluation context to GovernanceGates.check_all()
    → Gates return aggregated result; Engine updates DB

  Phase5BPromotionEngine ←→ AtomicPromotion
    → Engine delegates file + memory swap to AtomicPromotion
    → AtomicPromotion reports success/failure; Engine sets final status

  AtomicPromotion ←→ ModelRegistry
    → AtomicPromotion writes champion_registry.json
    → ModelRegistry reads from the same file on next load

  AtomicPromotion ←→ reload_predictor()
    → Direct function call after champion_registry.json is updated
    → New predictor loaded into memory immediately

  DriftGate ←→ v4_reference_distributions.json
    → Gate reads reference PSI distributions from JSON file at gate evaluation time

── 7. DATA FLOW ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

  Training Output
    → frequency_<v>.pkl + severity_<v>.pkl written to models/
    → challenger AUC, R², PSI computed and stored in promotion request

  Evaluation Request
    → {approval_id, challenger_metrics, bypass_flags}
    → ModelPromotion row created (EVALUATING)
    → GovernanceGates evaluates sequentially
    → evaluation_report JSONB populated with gates_passed, gates_failed

  Approved Promotion
    → champion_registry.json updated atomically
    → old snapshot → ModelPromotion.old_champion_snapshot (JSONB)
    → new snapshot → ModelPromotion.new_champion_snapshot (JSONB)
    → reload_predictor() swaps in-memory instance

  Rollback
    → old_champion_snapshot read → champion_registry.json restored
    → reload_predictor() reloads previous models
    → RollbackHistory row persisted with full audit trail

  Governance Log (append-only)
    → governance_log.json records every status transition
    → Readable by admin API for audit reporting

── 8. SUMMARY ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

  The Model Governance Architecture is a 6-phase lifecycle that enforces
  actuarial quality control before any model reaches production. Phase 5B
  is the gate engine: 7 sequential gates covering approval authorization,
  relative performance, absolute performance floors, shadow evidence, and
  drift. Promotion is atomic — champion_registry.json is swapped via
  os.replace() and the in-memory predictor is hot-reloaded without service
  restart. Rollback is always available via 6 trigger types. Every state
  transition is persisted in PostgreSQL (ModelPromotion, RollbackHistory)
  and in a governance_log.json audit file. The architecture guarantees no
  silent model degradation can reach users without passing all 7 gates.

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
SECTION 7 — AGENT ARCHITECTURE
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

── 1. OVERVIEW ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

  The system contains two distinct AI agents:

  AGENT A — Analytics Agent
    Framework : LangChain SQL agent
    LLM       : Groq Llama 3.3 70B (temp=0.1, max_tokens=512)
    Purpose   : Natural-language queries over PostgreSQL analytics tables
    Session   : Redis (TTL 3600s) with in-memory fallback (max 20 msgs)
    Access    : 6 allowed tables, mandatory LIMIT/aggregate on all SQL

  AGENT B — Underwriting Agent (HITL)
    Framework : LangGraph StateGraph
    LLM       : Groq Llama 3.3 70B (temp=0.15, max_tokens=350)
    Purpose   : Fraud assessment + underwriting decision with human review
    Interrupt : interrupt_before=["human_review"] — pauses for human input
    State     : PostgreSQL async checkpointer (thread_id per policy)
    Nodes     : fraud_detector → reasoning → decision → human_review

── 2. COMPONENTS ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────

  ANALYTICS AGENT COMPONENTS
  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │  AnalyticsAgent                                                  │
  │  InsuranceAssistant (NLP core)                                   │
  │  _RedisSessionMemory / _InMemorySessionMemory                    │
  │  ChatGroq (LLM client)                                           │
  │  SQLDatabase (LangChain, 6-table whitelist)                      │
  │  SQLDatabaseToolkit (provides sql_db_query tool)                 │
  │  INJECTION_PATTERNS (regex security filter)                      │
  │  BLOCKED_KEYWORDS (DML/DDL regex guard)                          │
  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  UNDERWRITING AGENT COMPONENTS
  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │  StateGraph (LangGraph)                                          │
  │  fraud_signal_node                                               │
  │  reasoning_node (LLM)                                            │
  │  decision_node (rule matrix)                                     │
  │  human_review_node (HITL)                                        │
  │  PostgreSQL async checkpointer                                   │
  │  ReviewQueuePanel (frontend polling UI)                          │
  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

── 3. STEP-BY-STEP FLOW ────────────────────────────────────────────────

  ANALYTICS AGENT FLOW

  Step 1 : User submits natural-language query via frontend chat
  Step 2 : POST /api/v1/analytics/query received by FastAPI
  Step 3 : AnalyticsAgent.run(session_id, query) called
  Step 4 : get_session_memory(session_id) — Redis ping; fallback if fail
  Step 5 : memory.get_history() — last ≤10 turns (20 messages max)
  Step 6 : classify_intent(query) — determines query category
  Step 7 : contextualize(query, history) — prepends conversation context
  Step 8 : InsuranceAssistant.query(contextualized_query)
             ├── INJECTION_PATTERNS check — HTTP 400 if injection detected
             ├── _build_agent() (lazy, once per process)
             │     ├── SQLDatabase(include_tables=ALLOWED_TABLES, sample_rows=0)
             │     ├── ChatGroq(llama-3.3-70b, temp=0, max_tokens=512)
             │     ├── SQLDatabaseToolkit(db, llm)
             │     └── create_sql_agent(max_iterations=10,
             │                          return_intermediate_steps=True)
             ├── agent.invoke(input, config) — LangChain agent loop
             │     ├── LLM generates SQL query
             │     ├── sql_db_query tool executes query
             │     ├── BLOCKED_KEYWORDS check on SQL string
             │     ├── LIMIT or aggregate mandatory (_validate_sql)
             │     └── Result returned to LLM for answer generation
             ├── Retry loop: attempts 0→1→2→3 with 0,1,2,4s backoff
             ├── _parse_sql_observation() extracts raw SQL + result
             ├── _suggest_chart_from_result() → [CHART: bar/line/pie]
             └── _format_answer() → structured response
  Step 9 : detect_anomalies(result) — flags outliers in result set
  Step 10: generate_recommendations(result) — analytical insights
  Step 11: memory.append(query, response) — persists to Redis/memory
  Step 12: AgentAnalyticsResponse returned to frontend

  UNDERWRITING AGENT FLOW

  Step 1 : Quote is submitted, underwriting flag triggered by risk level
  Step 2 : POST /api/v1/underwriting/evaluate
  Step 3 : get_underwriting_graph() retrieves compiled StateGraph
  Step 4 : graph.ainvoke(initial_state, config={thread_id: policy_id})
  Step 5 : NODE — fraud_signal_node
             ├── evaluate_fraud_signals(state) called
             ├── Records timing metrics
             └── Appends fraud_signals list to state
  Step 6 : NODE — reasoning_node
             ├── ChatGroq(temp=0.15, max_tokens=350) called
             ├── Prompt includes fraud signals + policy data
             ├── LLM generates underwriting reasoning text
             └── Falls back to _fallback_reasoning() if LLM fails
  Step 7 : NODE — decision_node (rule matrix)
             ├── high_signals >= 2 OR (high>=1 AND med>=2)
             │     → decision="refer", confidence=0.90, factor=×1.25
             ├── high_signals == 1 OR med_signals >= 2
             │     → decision="flag", confidence=0.78, factor=×1.15
             └── else
                   → decision="approve", confidence=0.95, factor=×1.0
  Step 8 : needs_human_review() check
             ├── confidence < 0.85 → True
             ├── fraud_signals present → True
             ├── claim_probability > 0.40 → True
             └── else → False (graph terminates, no HITL needed)
  Step 9 : interrupt_before=["human_review"] fires if HITL needed
             ├── Graph state saved to PostgreSQL checkpointer
             ├── Thread_id (policy_id) returned to frontend
             └── Frontend ReviewQueuePanel polls every 30s
  Step 10: Human reviewer views case in AdminDashboard ReviewQueuePanel
  Step 11: Human injects decision via POST /api/v1/underwriting/resume
             ├── human_decision injected into saved graph state
             └── graph.ainvoke() resumes from checkpoint
  Step 12: NODE — human_review_node executes
             ├── Reads injected human_decision from state
             ├── Merges with agent decision
             └── Produces final underwriting decision
  Step 13: Final decision persisted to PostgreSQL, returned to caller

── 4. DETAILED HIERARCHICAL FLOWCHART ─────────────────────────────────

ANALYTICS AGENT
═══════════════

  [Browser Chat UI]
       │
       ↓ HTTP POST /api/v1/analytics/query {session_id, query}
  [FastAPI Route]
       │
       ↓
  [AnalyticsAgent.run()]
       │
       ├─→ [get_session_memory(session_id)]
       │         │
       │         ├─→ Redis.ping() succeeds → _RedisSessionMemory
       │         │     key: "agent:analytics:<session_id>"
       │         │     TTL: 3600s, MAX_TURNS: 10 (20 msgs)
       │         └─→ Redis.ping() fails → _InMemorySessionMemory
       │               thread-safe (threading.Lock), same API
       │
       ├─→ [memory.get_history()] → List[HumanMessage, AIMessage]
       │
       ├─→ [classify_intent(query)] → intent_label
       │
       ├─→ [contextualize(query, history)] → enriched_query
       │
       ↓
  [InsuranceAssistant.query(enriched_query)]
       │
       ├─→ INJECTION CHECK
       │     INJECTION_PATTERNS.search(query)
       │     ├─→ Match found → raise HTTP 400 "Injection detected"
       │     └─→ No match → continue
       │
       ├─→ AGENT BUILD (lazy singleton)
       │     SQLDatabase(include_tables=[
       │       "customers","vehicles","driving_profiles",
       │       "policies","predictions","claims"
       │     ], sample_rows=0)
       │     ChatGroq(model="llama-3.3-70b-versatile", temp=0)
       │     SQLDatabaseToolkit(db, llm)
       │     create_sql_agent(max_iterations=10,
       │                       return_intermediate_steps=True)
       │
       ├─→ RETRY LOOP (attempts: 0,1,2,3 / delays: 0,1,2,4s)
       │     agent.invoke({"input": enriched_query})
       │       │
       │       ├─→ LLM thinks → generates SQL string
       │       │
       │       ├─→ sql_db_query tool executes SQL
       │       │     │
       │       │     ├─→ _validate_sql(sql)
       │       │     │     ├─→ BLOCKED_KEYWORDS match → raise ValueError
       │       │     │     │     (INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE/DROP/CREATE/ALTER)
       │       │     │     └─→ No LIMIT + no aggregate → raise ValueError
       │       │     │           "Query must include LIMIT or aggregate"
       │       │     │
       │       │     └─→ SQL executed on PostgreSQL
       │       │           result rows → LangChain tool observation
       │       │
       │       ├─→ LLM observes result → generates answer
       │       └─→ AgentFinish with output
       │
       ├─→ _parse_sql_observation() → {sql, raw_result}
       │
       ├─→ _suggest_chart_from_result() → "[CHART: bar]" or None
       │
       └─→ _format_answer() → {answer_text, sql, chart_hint}
       │
       ↓
  [AnalyticsAgent continues]
       │
       ├─→ detect_anomalies(result) → anomaly_flags
       │
       ├─→ generate_recommendations(result) → insight_list
       │
       ├─→ memory.append(query, response) → saved to Redis/memory
       │
       └─→ AgentAnalyticsResponse {answer, sql, chart, anomalies,
                                    recommendations, session_id}
       │
       ↓ HTTP 200
  [Browser Chat UI renders response]

UNDERWRITING AGENT (LangGraph HITL)
═════════════════════════════════════

  [Quote Created — risk_level triggers underwriting]
       │
       ↓ POST /api/v1/underwriting/evaluate
  [FastAPI Route]
       │
       ↓
  [get_underwriting_graph()]
       │
       ├─→ _graph not None → return existing compiled graph
       └─→ _graph is None
             setup_underwriting_graph()
               ├─→ setup_checkpointer() → AsyncPostgresCheckpointer
               ├─→ StateGraph(UnderwritingState)
               │     .add_node("fraud_detector", fraud_signal_node)
               │     .add_node("reasoning", reasoning_node)
               │     .add_node("decision", decision_node)
               │     .add_node("human_review", human_review_node)
               │     .add_edge(START, "fraud_detector")
               │     .add_edge("fraud_detector", "reasoning")
               │     .add_edge("reasoning", "decision")
               │     .add_conditional_edges("decision", route_fn)
               │     .add_edge("human_review", END)
               └─→ .compile(checkpointer=cp,
                             interrupt_before=["human_review"])
       │
       ↓
  [graph.ainvoke(state, config={thread_id: policy_id})]
       │
       ↓
  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │  NODE: fraud_detector                                           │
  │    evaluate_fraud_signals(state)                                │
  │    → scans policy data for anomaly patterns                     │
  │    → records elapsed_ms timing                                  │
  │    → state.fraud_signals = [Signal(level, description)]         │
  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
       │
       ↓
  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │  NODE: reasoning                                                │
  │    ChatGroq(temp=0.15, max_tokens=350)                          │
  │    Prompt: fraud_signals + policy + vehicle + driving data      │
  │    LLM generates: underwriting_reasoning (prose text)           │
  │    Fallback: _fallback_reasoning() if Groq call fails           │
  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
       │
       ↓
  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │  NODE: decision                                                 │
  │    COUNT high_signals, medium_signals from state.fraud_signals  │
  │    MATRIX:                                                      │
  │      high>=2 OR (high=1 AND med>=2)                             │
  │        → "refer" | confidence=0.90 | premium_factor=1.25        │
  │      high=1 OR med>=2                                           │
  │        → "flag"  | confidence=0.78 | premium_factor=1.15        │
  │      else                                                       │
  │        → "approve" | confidence=0.95 | premium_factor=1.0       │
  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
       │
       ↓
  [needs_human_review(state)?]
       │
       ├─→ FALSE: confidence>=0.85 AND no signals AND claim_prob<=0.40
       │     graph → END (no interrupt)
       │     decision persisted directly
       │
       └─→ TRUE: confidence<0.85 OR fraud_signals OR claim_prob>0.40
             │
             ↓ interrupt_before=["human_review"] fires
             │
  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │  GRAPH PAUSED                                                   │
  │    State snapshot saved → PostgreSQL checkpointer               │
  │    thread_id = policy_id (UUID)                                 │
  │    HTTP response: {status:"pending_review", thread_id}          │
  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
             │
             ↓ (async wait — human is reviewing)
             │
  [AdminDashboard ReviewQueuePanel]
       refetchInterval: 30000ms
       GET /api/v1/underwriting/queue → pending cases
       Human reviews: fraud_signals, reasoning, decision
             │
             ↓
  [Human submits decision]
       POST /api/v1/underwriting/resume
         {thread_id, human_decision: "approve"/"reject"/"refer"}
             │
             ↓
  [graph.ainvoke(injected_state, config={thread_id})]
       resumes from PostgreSQL checkpoint
             │
             ↓
  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │  NODE: human_review                                             │
  │    reads state.human_decision (injected by API)                 │
  │    merges with agent decision                                   │
  │    produces final_underwriting_decision                         │
  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
             │
             ↓ END
  [Final decision persisted to PostgreSQL]
  [HTTP 200 returned with final underwriting result]

── 5. COMPONENT RESPONSIBILITIES ──────────────────────────────────────

  AnalyticsAgent
    → Orchestrates query lifecycle (memory, contextualize, delegate)
    → Calls InsuranceAssistant and post-processes result
    → Manages session memory read/write

  InsuranceAssistant
    → Security gateway (injection check before any LLM call)
    → Builds and owns the LangChain SQL agent singleton
    → Implements retry logic with exponential backoff
    → SQL validation (blocked keywords, mandatory LIMIT)

  _RedisSessionMemory / _InMemorySessionMemory
    → Maintains per-session conversation history (max 20 messages)
    → Redis preferred (TTL 3600s); in-memory fallback on Redis failure
    → Thread-safe (Lock in in-memory implementation)

  fraud_signal_node
    → Stateless fraud heuristic evaluator
    → Writes fraud_signals list into LangGraph state
    → Records timing for observability

  reasoning_node
    → LLM call (Groq Llama 3.3 70B) for qualitative reasoning
    → Deterministic fallback (_fallback_reasoning) if LLM unavailable
    → Does not make final decision — feeds reasoning text to state

  decision_node
    → Pure rule matrix (no LLM)
    → Converts fraud signal counts to structured decision
    → Deterministic, auditable, reproducible

  human_review_node
    → Executes only after human injects state via resume API
    → Final authority node — merges human + agent decisions

  PostgreSQL checkpointer
    → Persists complete graph state between interrupt and resume
    → Identified by thread_id (= policy_id UUID)
    → Enables durable HITL workflows across HTTP requests

── 6. INTERACTION BETWEEN COMPONENTS ──────────────────────────────────

  AnalyticsAgent ←→ InsuranceAssistant
    → AnalyticsAgent.run() calls assistant.query()
    → Assistant returns structured response; Agent post-processes

  InsuranceAssistant ←→ LangChain SQL Agent
    → Agent is built once (lazy singleton); reused across calls
    → Agent calls sql_db_query → PostgreSQL → returns result to LLM

  AnalyticsAgent ←→ SessionMemory
    → get_history() before query (context injection)
    → append() after query (memory update)

  StateGraph nodes ←→ LangGraph state (UnderwritingState TypedDict)
    → Each node reads from and writes to shared state dict
    → State passed forward automatically by LangGraph

  decision_node ←→ human_review_node (conditional edge)
    → route_fn reads needs_human_review flag from state
    → Determines whether graph proceeds to human_review or END

  human_review_node ←→ PostgreSQL checkpointer
    → On interrupt: checkpointer saves state keyed by thread_id
    → On resume: checkpointer restores state; graph continues

  ReviewQueuePanel (frontend) ←→ Underwriting API
    → Polls GET /queue every 30s for pending HITL cases
    → Posts to /resume when human submits decision

── 7. DATA FLOW ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

  Analytics Agent Data Flow
    Query string → injection-checked → contextualized with history
    → SQL generated by LLM → executed on PostgreSQL
    → raw rows → parsed → chart-hinted → formatted answer
    → anomaly flags + recommendations appended
    → full response + session_id → Redis memory update

  Underwriting Agent Data Flow
    Policy data (customer, vehicle, driving, premium)
    → fraud_signals list appended
    → reasoning text appended
    → decision {label, confidence, factor} appended
    → if HITL: state serialized → PostgreSQL checkpoint
    → human_decision injected → state restored → merged
    → final_underwriting_decision persisted to DB

── 8. SUMMARY ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

  The system uses two complementary AI agents serving different purposes.
  The Analytics Agent uses LangChain's SQL agent pattern for reactive
  natural-language data querying — secured by injection filtering, SQL
  validation, and session memory. The Underwriting Agent uses LangGraph's
  interrupt-resume pattern for durable human-in-the-loop underwriting
  decisions — the graph pauses at human_review, saves to PostgreSQL,
  and resumes when a human submits their decision via the admin panel.
  Both agents use Groq Llama 3.3 70B as the LLM, but with different
  temperature and purpose: the Analytics Agent generates SQL queries,
  while the Underwriting Agent generates qualitative reasoning that feeds
  a deterministic rule matrix for the final decision.

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
SECTION 8 — SECURITY ARCHITECTURE
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

── 1. OVERVIEW ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

  Security is implemented at six distinct layers:
1. Authentication   : JWT HS256 (python-jose), 60-minute expiry
2. Credential check : secrets.compare_digest (timing-safe comparison)
3. Authorization    : role-based (admin role in JWT payload)
4. Transport        : CORS policy from settings.CORS_ORIGINS
5. SQL security     : ALLOWED_TABLES whitelist + BLOCKED_KEYWORDS + LIMIT
6. Prompt injection : INJECTION_PATTERNS regex on all LLM inputs
7. Error safety     : RFC 7807 Problem+JSON (no internal detail leakage)
8. Input validation : Pydantic schemas with field validators on all routes

── 2. COMPONENTS ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────

  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │  backend/src/auth/security.py                                    │
  │    create_access_token()    JWT creation                         │
  │    decode_token()           JWT verification + HTTP 401          │
  │    verify_credentials()     timing-safe username + password      │
  │                                                                  │
  │  backend/src/nlp_assistant/assistant.py                          │
  │    INJECTION_PATTERNS       9 regex patterns                     │
  │    BLOCKED_KEYWORDS         DML/DDL regex                        │
  │    ALLOWED_TABLES           6-element set                        │
  │    _validate_sql()          LIMIT/aggregate enforcement          │
  │                                                                  │
  │  backend/src/core/config.py                                      │
  │    CORS_ORIGINS             list of allowed origins              │
  │    SECRET_KEY               loaded from environment              │
  │                                                                  │
  │  backend/src/api/middleware.py                                   │
  │    HTTP request logging middleware                               │
  │                                                                  │
  │  Pydantic schemas (schemas.py)                                   │
  │    Field validators on all request bodies                        │
  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

── 3. STEP-BY-STEP FLOW ────────────────────────────────────────────────

  AUTHENTICATION FLOW (Admin Login)

  Step 1 : POST /api/v1/auth/login {username, password}
  Step 2 : verify_credentials(username, password)
             secrets.compare_digest(plain_username, settings.ADMIN_USERNAME)
             secrets.compare_digest(plain_password, settings.ADMIN_PASSWORD)
             (timing-safe — both sides compared even if username wrong)
             (passwords stored as plaintext in environment — no bcrypt)
  Step 3 : create_access_token(sub=username)
             payload = {sub, role:"admin", iat, exp: now+60min}
             jwt.encode(payload, settings.SECRET_KEY, algorithm="HS256")
  Step 4 : Token returned in response body
  Step 5 : Frontend stores token in localStorage key "admin_token"

  PROTECTED ROUTE FLOW

  Step 1 : Request arrives with Authorization: Bearer <token>
  Step 2 : decode_token(token)
             jwt.decode(token, settings.SECRET_KEY, algorithms=["HS256"])
             JWTError → HTTP 401 Unauthorized
             Expired → HTTP 401 Unauthorized
  Step 3 : role checked from decoded payload
  Step 4 : Route handler executes

  SQL SECURITY FLOW (Analytics / NLP queries)

  Step 1 : User submits natural-language query
  Step 2 : INJECTION_PATTERNS.search(query)
             9 regex patterns covering:
               ignore previous instructions
               system prompt
               jailbreak keywords
               role play as
               forget your instructions
               act as [role]
               disregard [rules]
               override [safety]
               reveal [prompt/instructions]
             → Match → HTTP 400, query rejected
  Step 3 : LangChain agent generates SQL
  Step 4 : validate_sql(generated_sql)
             BLOCKED_KEYWORDS check:
               INSERT|UPDATE|DELETE|DROP|CREATE|ALTER|TRUNCATE
               GRANT|REVOKE|EXECUTE|EXEC|sp|xp_
             → Match → ValueError, SQL rejected, retry triggered
  Step 5 : Table access check (implicit via SQLDatabase config)
             SQLDatabase(include_tables=ALLOWED_TABLES)
             ALLOWED_TABLES = {customers, vehicles, driving_profiles,
                                policies, predictions, claims}
             Tables not in whitelist are invisible to the agent
  Step 6 : LIMIT/aggregate enforcement
             if "LIMIT" not in sql.upper()
               and no aggregate (COUNT/SUM/AVG/MIN/MAX)
               → ValueError "Query must include LIMIT or aggregate"
  Step 7 : SQL executed on PostgreSQL (read-only in practice)

  CORS FLOW

  Step 1 : Browser preflight OPTIONS request
  Step 2 : FastAPI CORSMiddleware checks Origin header
             against settings.CORS_ORIGINS list
  Step 3 : Origin allowed → Access-Control-Allow-Origin header added
  Step 4 : Origin not allowed → no CORS headers → browser blocks request

  ERROR SAFETY FLOW

  Step 1 : Exception raised anywhere in route handler
  Step 2 : Global exception handler intercepts
  Step 3 : RFC 7807 Problem+JSON response built:
             {type, title, status, detail}
             No stack traces, no internal paths, no model details
  Step 4 : HTTP 4xx/5xx returned to client

── 4. DETAILED HIERARCHICAL FLOWCHART ─────────────────────────────────

SECURITY LAYERS — TOP TO BOTTOM
════════════════════════════════

  [Incoming HTTP Request]
       │
       ↓
  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │  LAYER 1: CORS MIDDLEWARE                                       │
  │    Origin header checked against CORS_ORIGINS (settings)        │
  │    ├─→ Origin allowed → continue to next middleware             │
  │    └─→ Origin not in list → 403 Forbidden (browser blocked)     │
  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
       │
       ↓
  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │  LAYER 2: HTTP REQUEST LOGGING MIDDLEWARE                       │
  │    Logs: method, path, status_code, elapsed_ms                  │
  │    No sensitive data logged (no auth headers, no body)          │
  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
       │
       ↓
  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │  LAYER 3: JWT AUTHENTICATION (protected routes only)            │
  │    Dependency: get_current_user = Depends(decode_token)         │
  │    Authorization: Bearer <JWT>                                  │
  │    jwt.decode(token, SECRET_KEY, algorithms=["HS256"])          │
  │    ├─→ Valid + not expired → user payload injected into route   │
  │    ├─→ Expired → HTTP 401 "Token has expired"                  │
  │    └─→ Invalid sig → HTTP 401 "Could not validate credentials"  │
  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
       │
       ↓
  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │  LAYER 4: PYDANTIC INPUT VALIDATION                             │
  │    All request bodies validated against Pydantic models         │
  │    CustomerQuoteRequest validators:                             │
  │      gender   → .lower() normalization                          │
  │      city     → .strip().lower()                               │
  │      brand    → .strip().lower()                               │
  │      segment  → canonical map (e.g. "suv"→"SUV")               │
  │      fuel     → canonical map (e.g. "petrol"→"Petrol")          │
  │    ├─→ Valid → route handler receives clean data               │
  │    └─→ Invalid → HTTP 422 Unprocessable Entity (Pydantic error) │
  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
       │
       ↓
  [ROUTE HANDLER EXECUTES]
       │
       │ (for /analytics and /nlp routes only)
       ↓
  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │  LAYER 5: PROMPT INJECTION FILTER                               │
  │    INJECTION_PATTERNS.search(user_query)                        │
  │    Patterns (9):                                                │
  │      r"ignore (all )?(previous|prior) instructions?"            │
  │      r"system prompt"                                           │
  │      r"jailbreak"                                               │
  │      r"role.?play as"                                           │
  │      r"forget (your|all) instructions?"                         │
  │      r"act as (a |an )?\w+"                                     │
  │      r"disregard (your |all )?(rules|instructions)"             │
  │      r"override (safety|guidelines|rules)"                      │
  │      r"reveal (your |the )?(prompt|instructions|system)"        │
  │    ├─→ No match → query proceeds to LLM                         │
  │    └─→ Match → HTTP 400 "Potentially harmful query detected"    │
  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
       │
       ↓
  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │  LAYER 6: SQL SECURITY (generated SQL validation)               │
  │                                                                 │
  │  6A — TABLE WHITELIST                                           │
  │    SQLDatabase(include_tables={                                  │
  │      "customers","vehicles","driving_profiles",                 │
  │      "policies","predictions","claims"})                        │
  │    → 17 total DB tables; only 6 visible to LLM agent            │
  │    → model_promotions, shadow_predictions, etc. never accessed  │
  │                                                                 │
  │  6B — BLOCKED KEYWORDS                                          │
  │    re.compile(r"\b(INSERT|UPDATE|DELETE|DROP|CREATE|ALTER|      │
  │      TRUNCATE|GRANT|REVOKE|EXECUTE|EXEC|sp_|xp_)\b", re.I)     │
  │    ├─→ Match in SQL → ValueError → retry with corrective prompt │
  │    └─→ No match → proceed                                       │
  │                                                                 │
  │  6C — LIMIT / AGGREGATE ENFORCEMENT                             │
  │    if "LIMIT" not in sql.upper()                                │
  │       and not re.search(r"\b(COUNT|SUM|AVG|MIN|MAX)\b", sql, I) │
  │    → ValueError "Query must include LIMIT or aggregate"         │
  │    (prevents unbounded table scans)                             │
  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
       │
       ↓
  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │  LAYER 7: RFC 7807 ERROR HANDLER (all routes)                   │
  │    Exception intercepted by global handler                      │
  │    Response: Content-Type: application/problem+json             │
  │    Body: {                                                       │
  │      "type": "about:blank",                                     │
  │      "title": "Error Title",                                    │
  │      "status": 4xx/5xx,                                         │
  │      "detail": "safe user-facing message"                       │
  │    }                                                             │
  │    No stack trace, no file path, no DB schema, no model path    │
  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
       │
       ↓
  [Response returned to client]

AUTH SUBSYSTEM DETAIL
═════════════════════

  POST /api/v1/auth/login
       │
       ↓
  [verify_credentials(username, password)]
       │
       ├─→ secrets.compare_digest(
       │       input_username.encode(),
       │       settings.ADMIN_USERNAME.encode()
       │   )  ← timing-safe: always runs full comparison
       │
       ├─→ secrets.compare_digest(
       │       input_password.encode(),
       │       settings.ADMIN_PASSWORD.encode()
       │   )  ← timing-safe: prevents username enumeration
       │
       ├─→ Both match → create_access_token()
       │     payload = {
       │       "sub": username,
       │       "role": "admin",
       │       "iat": datetime.utcnow(),
       │       "exp": datetime.utcnow() + timedelta(minutes=60)
       │     }
       │     jwt.encode(payload, SECRET_KEY, algorithm="HS256")
       │     → token string returned
       │
       └─→ Either mismatch → HTTP 401 "Invalid credentials"

── 5. COMPONENT RESPONSIBILITIES ──────────────────────────────────────

  security.py
    → Sole authority for token creation and verification
    → create_access_token: signs payload, sets 60-minute expiry
    → decode_token: verifies signature, raises 401 on any failure
    → verify_credentials: timing-safe comparison for both fields

  INJECTION_PATTERNS (assistant.py)
    → First gate before any user text reaches the LLM
    → 9 patterns targeting known prompt injection techniques
    → Stateless regex — no LLM involvement in this check

  BLOCKED_KEYWORDS (assistant.py)
    → Guards against LLM generating destructive SQL
    → Applied to generated SQL before execution
    → Case-insensitive, word-boundary matched

  ALLOWED_TABLES (assistant.py)
    → Structural isolation — LLM cannot see or query protected tables
    → Enforced at SQLDatabase construction (LangChain)

  _validate_sql() (assistant.py)
    → Final SQL safety check
    → Prevents unbounded queries that could exhaust DB connections

  CORSMiddleware (FastAPI built-in)
    → Reads CORS_ORIGINS from pydantic_settings
    → Prevents cross-origin requests from unauthorized domains

  RFC 7807 error handler
    → Sanitizes all exceptions before returning to client
    → Prevents information leakage via error messages

── 6. INTERACTION BETWEEN COMPONENTS ──────────────────────────────────

  CORS → JWT → Pydantic → Route Handler
    → Sequential middleware/dependency chain on every request
    → Each layer can reject the request before the next runs

  Route Handler → Injection Filter → SQL Security → PostgreSQL
    → For analytics routes: user query passes injection filter
    → LLM generates SQL, SQL passes blocked-keyword and limit checks
    → Only then does SQL execute against PostgreSQL

  verify_credentials → create_access_token → JWT stored in frontend
    → Login is the only path to obtain a valid JWT
    → All subsequent admin actions require Bearer token

  decode_token → FastAPI Depends()
    → Injected as dependency into every protected route
    → Returns user payload or raises 401 before route body runs

── 7. DATA FLOW ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

  Credentials flow
    → POST body {username, password} (plaintext over HTTPS)
    → secrets.compare_digest (constant-time)
    → JWT payload signed with SECRET_KEY (HS256)
    → Token string in response → localStorage in browser

  Protected request flow
    → Authorization: Bearer <jwt> header
    → decode_token() → payload dict injected into route
    → Route executes with authenticated context

  NLP query flow
    → User text → INJECTION_PATTERNS (regex check)
    → Groq LLM generates SQL → BLOCKED_KEYWORDS check
    → LIMIT validation → PostgreSQL execution (6 tables only)
    → Result → formatted response (no raw SQL in response by default)

  Error flow
    → Any exception → RFC 7807 handler → sanitized JSON response
    → HTTP status code preserved, internal details stripped

── 8. SUMMARY ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

  Security is layered across 7 distinct enforcement points. Authentication
  uses JWT HS256 with 60-minute expiry and timing-safe credential comparison
  (secrets.compare_digest). SQL agent access is triply protected: an
  ALLOWED_TABLES whitelist at construction time, BLOCKED_KEYWORDS on
  generated SQL, and a mandatory LIMIT/aggregate requirement. Prompt
  injection is blocked by 9 regex patterns before any text reaches the LLM.
  All errors return RFC 7807 Problem+JSON to prevent information leakage.
  The one notable gap: passwords are stored as plaintext in environment
  variables (no bcrypt hashing), which is an acknowledged design constraint
  of the current implementation.

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
SECTION 9 — REQUEST LIFECYCLE
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

── 1. OVERVIEW ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

  The request lifecycle traces the complete path of a V2 quote request —
  the primary user journey — from browser form submission to database
  persistence and structured JSON response. It crosses: React frontend,
  Axios HTTP client, FastAPI middleware chain, Pydantic validation,
  driving score engine, ML predictor, premium calculation engine,
  SQLAlchemy ORM persistence, shadow deployment background task, and
  the JSON response construction path.

── 2. COMPONENTS ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────

  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │  FRONTEND                                                       │
  │    QuoteFormV2 (5-step wizard, 18 fields)                       │
  │    React Hook Form + Zod schema validation                      │
  │    VehicleModelCombobox, DerivedFeaturesPanel                   │
  │    lib/insuranceUtils.ts (VEHICLE_BASE_INR, estimateVehicleValue)│
  │    apiClientV2 (Axios instance, baseURL=/api/v2)                │
  │    TanStack Query useMutation                                   │
  │                                                                 │
  │  TRANSPORT                                                      │
  │    HTTP POST /api/v2/quote                                       │
  │    JSON body: QuoteRequestV2 (18 fields)                        │
  │                                                                 │
  │  BACKEND MIDDLEWARE                                             │
  │    CORSMiddleware (origin check)                                │
  │    HTTP request logging middleware                              │
  │    No auth required on /quote (public endpoint)                 │
  │                                                                 │
  │  BACKEND ROUTE (quote_v2.py)                                    │
  │    Pydantic schema validation                                   │
  │    enrich_v2_request()                                          │
  │    calculate_driving_score()                                    │
  │    CompoundActuarialPredictor.predict()                         │
  │    PremiumCalculationEngine.calculate()                         │
  │    ShadowDeploymentService.record_background()                  │
  │    SQLAlchemy ORM persistence                                   │
  │    QuoteResponseV2 construction                                 │
  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

── 3. STEP-BY-STEP FLOW ────────────────────────────────────────────────

  Step 1 : User completes 5-step wizard in browser
  Step 2 : React Hook Form validates all 18 fields against Zod schema
  Step 3 : lib/insuranceUtils.ts computes derived fields client-side:
             estimateVehicleValue(brand, segment, year, fuel)
             deriveNcbPct(prior_claims, ncb_years)
             driving_score estimated for DerivedFeaturesPanel display
  Step 4 : apiClientV2.post('/quote', payload) via TanStack useMutation
  Step 5 : HTTP POST /api/v2/quote arrives at FastAPI
  Step 6 : CORSMiddleware checks Origin header
  Step 7 : HTTP logging middleware records request start
  Step 8 : FastAPI router dispatches to create_quote_v2()
  Step 9 : Pydantic QuoteRequestV2 validates body (HTTP 422 if invalid)
  Step 10: enrich_v2_request(request) called
             Derives vehicle_value if not provided
             Derives ncb_pct from claim history if not provided
             Normalizes city → tier (CITY_TIER_MAP lookup)
             Computes final driving_score if not supplied
  Step 11: calculate_driving_score() called with all 14 factors
             Base score = 50
             14 adjustments applied
             Result: {"score": 0-100, "factors": [...]}
  Step 12: predictor.predict(input_data)
             ├── DataCleaner.transform() — impute, clip outliers
             ├── FeatureEngineer.transform() — 12 derived features
             ├── InsurancePreprocessor.transform() — 33 features assembled
             │     5 StandardScaled + 2 MinMaxScaled + 13 OHE
             │     1 LabelEncoded + 12 Passthrough
             ├── FrequencyModel.predict_proba() → P(claim) ∈ [0,1]
             ├── SeverityModel.predict() → E[amount|claim] in INR
             ├── Expected Loss = P(claim) × E[amount]
             └── SHAP TreeExplainer → feature_contributions dict
  Step 13: engine.calculate(expected_loss, no_claim_bonus_pct=x)
             Base Premium = Expected Loss × 1.35 (loading factor)
             9 sequential business rule adjustments applied
             Floor: ₹3,000 | Ceiling: ₹1,50,000
             Risk classification: <0.20→low, <0.40→medium, ≥0.40→high
             Final premium INR value produced
  Step 14: ORM persistence (async SQLAlchemy session)
             Customer row (or upsert)
             Vehicle row
             DrivingProfile row
             Policy row
             Prediction row (with explanation_json SHAP, inference_features_json)
             V4 columns populated: champion_version, shadow_flag, etc.
  Step 15: ShadowDeploymentService.record_background()
             FastAPI BackgroundTask — fires after response sent
             Compares current challenger (if any) predictions
             Writes to shadow_predictions table
  Step 16: QuoteResponseV2 constructed:
             {premium_inr, risk_level, driving_score, ncb_pct,
              feature_contributions (SHAP), enriched_features,
              champion_metadata {version, algorithm, trained_at}}
  Step 17: HTTP 200 JSON response returned to browser
  Step 18: TanStack Query mutation.onSuccess fires
  Step 19: Browser displays: premium, risk badge, SHAP chart, NCB display

── 4. DETAILED HIERARCHICAL FLOWCHART ─────────────────────────────────

  [BROWSER — QuoteFormV2 (5-step wizard)]
       │
       ├─→ Step 1: Personal Info (name, age, gender, city, occupation)
       ├─→ Step 2: Vehicle Info (brand, model, segment, year, fuel, usage)
       ├─→ Step 3: Driving History (prior_claims, years_licensed, mileage)
       ├─→ Step 4: Coverage Options (parking_type, marital_status)
       └─→ Step 5: Review + Submit
       │
       ↓ React Hook Form validates 18-field Zod schema
       │  ├─→ Validation error → inline field errors shown, submit blocked
       │  └─→ Valid → proceed
       │
       ↓ lib/insuranceUtils.ts
       │  estimateVehicleValue(brand, segment, year, fuel) → INR estimate
       │  deriveNcbPct(prior_claims, ncb_years) → percentage
       │  (DerivedFeaturesPanel updates in real-time during form entry)
       │
       ↓ apiClientV2.post('/quote', formData)  [Axios, baseURL=/api/v2]
       │
  ═════════════════ HTTP BOUNDARY ══════════════════
       │
       ↓ POST /api/v2/quote   HTTP/1.1
       │  Content-Type: application/json
       │  Body: QuoteRequestV2 {18 fields}
       │
  [FastAPI — CORSMiddleware]
       ├─→ Origin in CORS_ORIGINS → continue
       └─→ Origin not allowed → 403 (browser-side block)
       │
       ↓
  [FastAPI — HTTPLoggingMiddleware]
       record(method="POST", path="/api/v2/quote", start_time)
       │
       ↓
  [FastAPI Router → create_quote_v2(request: QuoteRequestV2)]
       │
       ↓ Pydantic validation
       │  ├─→ Field error → HTTP 422 {detail: [...validation errors]}
       │  └─→ Valid → request object populated
       │
       ↓
  [enrich_v2_request(request)]
       │
       ├─→ vehicle_value missing?
       │     estimateVehicleValue() → derive from brand/segment/year/fuel
       │
       ├─→ ncb_pct missing?
       │     deriveNcbPct() → derive from claim history
       │
       ├─→ city_tier
       │     CITY_TIER_MAP.get(city.lower(), "Tier-3")
       │     Tier-1: 8 cities (Mumbai, Delhi, Bangalore, etc.)
       │     Tier-2: 12 cities (Pune, Jaipur, Lucknow, etc.)
       │     Tier-3: everything else
       │
       └─→ driving_score override if not supplied (calculated below)
       │
       ↓
  [calculate_driving_score(14 factors)]
       │
       base_score = 50
       ├─→ prior_claims:       0→+10, 1→−5, 2→−15, 3+→−25
       ├─→ annual_mileage:     <5000→+5, 5-15k→0, 15-30k→−5, >30k→−10
       ├─→ years_licensed:     <2→−10, 2-5→−5, 5-10→0, >10→+10
       ├─→ age:                <25→−10, 25-60→+5, >60→−5
       ├─→ occupation:         professional→+5, student→−5, other→0
       ├─→ marital_status:     married→+5, single/other→0
       ├─→ vehicle_age:        <3→+5, 3-7→0, 7-10→−5, >10→−10
       ├─→ vehicle_value:      >15L→−5, 5-15L→0, <5L→+5
       ├─→ fuel_type:          EV→+10, Petrol→0, CNG→+5, Diesel→−5
       ├─→ vehicle_segment:    Compact→+5, SUV→−5, Luxury→−10, other→0
       ├─→ usage_type:         Personal→+5, Commercial→−10, Rideshare→−15
       ├─→ parking_type:       Garage→+10, Compound→+5, Street→−10
       ├─→ ncb_pct:            >40%→+10, 20-40%→+5, <20%→0
       └─→ city_risk:          low→+5, medium→0, high→−10
       final_score = max(0, min(100, base + sum(deltas)))
       → {"score": int, "factors": [{name, delta, explanation}]}
       │
       ↓
  [CompoundActuarialPredictor.predict(input_data)]
       │
       ├─→ DataCleaner.transform()
       │     Fill missing numerics with fitted medians
       │     Fill missing categoricals with fitted modes
       │     Clip outlier values to fitted bounds
       │
       ├─→ FeatureEngineer.transform()
       │     12 derived features:
       │       claim_rate_proxy, risk_score_composite,
       │       vehicle_age_mileage_interaction,
       │       high_risk_occupation_flag, premium_segment,
       │       city_vehicle_risk, ncb_driving_composite,
       │       young_driver_flag, high_value_vehicle_flag,
       │       commercial_use_flag, ev_bonus_flag,
       │       experience_age_ratio
       │     Column aliasing (standardize names)
       │     Normalization (city→tier, fuel/segment canonical)
       │
       ├─→ InsurancePreprocessor.transform()
       │     StandardScaler × 5 features
       │     MinMaxScaler × 2 features
       │     OneHotEncoder × 13 categorical features (sparse=False)
       │     LabelEncoder × 1 feature
       │     Passthrough × 12 features
       │     → 33-feature numpy array
       │
       ├─→ FrequencyModel (XGBClassifier)
       │     predict_proba(X)[:, 1]
       │     → claim_probability P(claim) ∈ [0.0, 1.0]
       │
       ├─→ SeverityModel (XGBRegressor or CatBoostSeverityWrapper)
       │     Active model determined by champion_registry.json
       │     XGBRegressor: predict() on log1p-transformed space → expm1()
       │     CatBoostWrapper: predict() directly (no log transform)
       │     → expected_severity E[amount|claim] in INR
       │
       ├─→ Expected Loss = claim_probability × expected_severity
       │
       └─→ shap.TreeExplainer(frequency_model).shap_values(X)
             → feature_contributions {feature_name: shap_value}
             → top 10 contributors selected for response
       │
       ↓
  [PremiumCalculationEngine.calculate(expected_loss, ncb_pct)]
       │
       base_premium = expected_loss × 1.35
       │
       Adjustment 1: driving_score_factor
         score≥80→×0.90, score≥60→×0.95, score≥40→×1.0,
         score≥20→×1.05, else→×1.15
       Adjustment 2: vehicle_age_loading
         age>10→×1.20, age>7→×1.10, age>3→×1.05, else×1.0
       Adjustment 3: annual_mileage_loading
         >30000→×1.15, >15000→×1.05, else×1.0
       Adjustment 4: usage_type_loading
         Commercial→×1.25, Rideshare→×1.30, else×1.0
       Adjustment 5: city_tier_loading
         Tier-1→×1.15, Tier-2→×1.05, Tier-3→×1.0
       Adjustment 6: fuel_type_loading
         EV→×0.85, CNG→×0.95, else×1.0
       Adjustment 7: no_claim_bonus_discount
         ncb_pct=0%→×1.0, 20%→×0.80, 25%→×0.75,
         35%→×0.70, 45%→×0.65, 50%→×0.60
       Adjustment 8: occupation_risk
         professional→×0.95, student→×1.10, else×1.0
       Adjustment 9: marital_status_adjustment
         married→×0.95, else×1.0
       │
       floor:   max(premium, 3000)
       ceiling: min(premium, 150000)
       risk_level: <0.20→"low", <0.40→"medium", ≥0.40→"high"
       → {final_premium_inr, risk_level, adjustment_breakdown}
       │
       ↓
  [SQLAlchemy async session — ORM persistence]
       │
       ├─→ Customer.create_or_update(name, age, gender, city, occupation)
       ├─→ Vehicle.create(brand, model, segment, year, fuel, value)
       ├─→ DrivingProfile.create(score, mileage, claims, licensed_years)
       ├─→ Policy.create(premium_inr, risk_level, coverage_type)
       └─→ Prediction.create(
               claim_probability, expected_severity, expected_loss,
               explanation_json=shap_values (JSONB),
               inference_features_json=preprocessed_features (JSONB),
               champion_version, champion_algorithm,
               shadow_flag=False
           )
       │
       ↓
  [FastAPI BackgroundTask — ShadowDeploymentService.record_background()]
       Fires AFTER response is sent (non-blocking)
       If challenger model exists:
         challenger.predict(same_input)
         shadow_predictions.insert(challenger_result, champion_result)
       Used as evidence for Governance Gate 6
       │
       ↓
  [QuoteResponseV2 constructed]
       {
         "premium_inr": int,
         "risk_level": "low"|"medium"|"high",
         "driving_score": {"score": int, "factors": [...]},
         "ncb_pct": float,
         "claim_probability": float,
         "expected_severity": float,
         "feature_contributions": {top 10 SHAP values},
         "enriched_features": {derived fields used in prediction},
         "champion_metadata": {
           "version": str,
           "algorithm": "xgboost"|"catboost",
           "trained_at": ISO datetime
         }
       }
       │
       ↓ HTTP 200 application/json
  ═════════════════ HTTP BOUNDARY ══════════════════
       │
       ↓ apiClientV2.post() resolves
       │
  [TanStack Query mutation.onSuccess(data)]
       │
       ├─→ Navigate to /quote/result?id=<policy_id>
       ├─→ Display premium in large format
       ├─→ Render risk badge (low/medium/high)
       ├─→ Render SHAP contributions bar chart (Recharts)
       ├─→ Display NCB discount applied
       └─→ Display driving score gauge

── 5. COMPONENT RESPONSIBILITIES ──────────────────────────────────────

  QuoteFormV2 (frontend)
    → Collects and validates 18 fields across 5 steps
    → Computes derived fields locally for real-time UX feedback
    → Submits via apiClientV2 (dedicated Axios instance for V2 API)

  enrich_v2_request() (backend)
    → Fills gaps in the request (vehicle_value, ncb_pct, city_tier)
    → Ensures downstream components always receive complete data

  calculate_driving_score()
    → Single-function, 14-factor scoring engine
    → Returns normalized 0-100 score + human-readable factor breakdown

  CompoundActuarialPredictor
    → Two-model compound: frequency × severity
    → Owns full preprocessing pipeline (DataCleaner → FeatureEngineer → Preprocessor)
    → Returns claim probability, severity, expected loss, SHAP contributions

  PremiumCalculationEngine
    → Pure business rules (no ML)
    → 9 sequential multipliers + floor + ceiling
    → Deterministic and auditable

  SQLAlchemy ORM layer
    → Async session management
    → Persists full audit trail: Customer + Vehicle + DrivingProfile + Policy + Prediction
    → SHAP values and inference features stored as JSONB for explainability audit

  ShadowDeploymentService
    → BackgroundTask — never delays response
    → Silently compares champion vs challenger predictions
    → Feeds Governance Gate 6 evidence

── 6. INTERACTION BETWEEN COMPONENTS ──────────────────────────────────

  Frontend → Backend
    → Zod validates before send; Pydantic validates on receive
    → Two separate validation layers catch schema errors

  enrich_v2_request → predict → calculate
    → Sequential pipeline: enrichment must complete before prediction
    → Prediction output (expected_loss) feeds engine input

  Predictor → ORM
    → Prediction row stores both the ML output AND the SHAP explanation
    → inference_features_json preserves preprocessed input for audit

  calculate() → ORM
    → Premium stored in Policy row; risk_level in both Policy and Prediction

  BackgroundTask timing
    → ShadowDeploymentService fires after HTTP response is sent
    → User never waits for shadow comparison — pure observability

── 7. DATA FLOW ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

  User form data (18 fields)
    → Zod validated → POST body → Pydantic validated
    → enrich_v2_request() adds vehicle_value, ncb_pct, city_tier
    → 14-factor driving score computed (base=50, adjustments applied)
    → DataCleaner → FeatureEngineer → Preprocessor → 33 features
    → FrequencyModel → P(claim) float
    → SeverityModel → E[amount] float
    → Expected Loss = P(claim) × E[amount]
    → SHAP → feature_contributions dict
    → PremiumCalculationEngine → final_premium_inr (floor/ceiling applied)
    → SQLAlchemy ORM → 5 DB rows written (async)
    → QuoteResponseV2 JSON → HTTP 200 → browser
    → ShadowDeploymentService → shadow_predictions row (async background)

── 8. SUMMARY ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

  The V2 request lifecycle is a 19-step pipeline spanning browser to
  database. The frontend handles schema validation and UX-level derived
  fields before submission. The backend applies a layered enrichment →
  scoring → ML prediction → business rules → persistence pipeline. The
  two-model actuarial architecture (FrequencyModel × SeverityModel) is
  the core computation; SHAP provides explainability. Shadow deployment
  runs as a non-blocking background task, preserving response latency
  while building model governance evidence. Every request produces 5
  persisted ORM rows with full audit trails including raw SHAP values
  and preprocessed feature vectors in JSONB columns.

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
SECTION 10 — PROJECT DIRECTORY ARCHITECTURE
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

── 1. OVERVIEW ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

  The project is a monorepo with two top-level packages: backend (Python
  FastAPI) and frontend (React TypeScript). A models directory holds
  trained model artifacts and metadata outside both packages. The
  directory structure reflects a deliberate domain separation: API surface
  in api/, ML pipeline in models/ + preprocessing/ + engine/, agent logic
  in agents/, governance in promotions/, database in db/, utilities in
  core/ + auth/.

── 2. COMPONENTS ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────

  Top-level directories:
    backend/         Python FastAPI application
    frontend/        React TypeScript application
    models/          Trained model artifacts + metadata + governance logs
    alembic/         Database migration scripts (001–010)
    .env             Environment configuration (not committed)

── 3. STEP-BY-STEP FLOW (Directory Traversal) ──────────────────────────

  How code flows through the directory structure on a quote request:

1. frontend/src/pages/QuoteFormV2Page.tsx — user fills form
2. frontend/src/lib/insuranceUtils.ts — derived fields computed
3. frontend/src/services/api.ts — HTTP request dispatched
4. backend/src/api/routes/quote_v2.py — request received
5. backend/src/api/schemas.py — Pydantic validation
6. backend/src/engine/driving_score.py — score computed
7. backend/src/models/predictor.py — ML prediction entry point
8. backend/src/preprocessing/pipeline.py — feature engineering
9. backend/src/models/frequency_model.py — P(claim)
10. backend/src/models/severity_model.py — E[amount]
11. backend/src/engine/calculator.py — premium rules
12. backend/src/db/models.py — ORM row construction
13. backend/src/db/session.py — async DB session
14. models/metadata/champion_registry.json — active model paths read
15. models/frequency_<version>.pkl — model file loaded
16. models/severity_<version>.pkl — model file loaded

── 4. DETAILED HIERARCHICAL FLOWCHART ─────────────────────────────────

PROJECT ROOT

│   │     Entry point. FastAPI app instantiation.

│   │       setup_underwriting_graph → APScheduler start

│   │     Router registration: v1 + v2 routes

│       │

│       │   │   │     Calls: calculate_driving_score, predictor.predict,

│       │   │   │     Admin dashboard data routes (JWT protected)

│       │   │   │

│       │   │   │     Model management API (list, promote, rollback)
│       │   │   │

│       │   │   │     Policy CRUD routes

│       │   │   │     Claims submission and tracking routes

│       │   │     Saved as severity_<version>.pkl / .cbm
│       │   │

│       │   │     ModelRegistry — reads/writes champion_registry.json
│       │   │     rollback_champion(), activate_champion()
│       │   │     Atomic file writes via os.replace()
│       │   │
│       │   └── training/
│       │         train_frequency.py  — full training pipeline for frequency
│       │         train_severity.py   — full training pipeline for severity
│       │         evaluate.py         — model evaluation utilities
│       │
│       ├── preprocessing/
│       │   └── pipeline.py
│       │         DataCleaner — fit medians/modes, transform (impute, clip)
│       │         FeatureEngineer — 12 derived features + aliasing + norm
│       │         InsurancePreprocessor — 33-feature assembly
│       │           (StandardScaler×5, MinMax×2, OHE×13, LE×1, Pass×12)
│       │         CITY_TIER_MAP, TOP_BRANDS constants
│       │         prepare_training_data() — 70/15/15 stratified split
│       │
│       ├── engine/
│       │   ├── calculator.py
│       │   │     PremiumCalculationEngine.calculate()
│       │   │     9 business rule adjustments + floor + ceiling
│       │   │     Expected Loss × 1.35 loading factor
│       │   │
│       │   └── driving_score.py
│       │         calculate_driving_score() — 14 factors, base=50
│       │         Returns score (0-100) + factor breakdown list
│       │
│       ├── agents/
│       │   ├── analytics/
│       │   │   ├── agent.py
│       │   │   │     AnalyticsAgent — orchestrates NLP query lifecycle
│       │   │   │     classify_intent, contextualize, post-process
│       │   │   │
│       │   │   └── memory.py
│       │   │         RedisSessionMemory (Redis, TTL=3600, MAX_TURNS=10)
│       │   │         InMemorySessionMemory (threading.Lock fallback)
│       │   │         get_session_memory() factory
│       │   │
│       │   └── underwriting/
│       │       ├── graph.py
│       │       │     LangGraph StateGraph compilation
│       │       │     setup_underwriting_graph() — called in lifespan
│       │       │     interrupt_before=["human_review"]
│       │       │
│       │       └── nodes.py
│       │             fraud_signal_node — heuristic fraud signals
│       │             reasoning_node — Groq LLM (temp=0.15, max_tokens=350)
│       │             decision_node — rule matrix (refer/flag/approve)
│       │             human_review_node — HITL merge
│       │             needs_human_review() — routing predicate
│       │
│       ├── nlp_assistant/
│       │   └── assistant.py
│       │         InsuranceAssistant — LangChain SQL agent
│       │         ALLOWED_TABLES (6), BLOCKED_KEYWORDS, INJECTION_PATTERNS
│       │         build_agent() — lazy singleton
│       │         query() — inject check → retry loop → validate SQL
│       │         SYSTEM_PROMPT — mandatory LIMIT 100 instruction
│       │
│       ├── promotions/
│       │   ├── engine.py
│       │   │     Phase5BPromotionEngine — evaluate() / execute() / rollback()
│       │   │     Status machine: EVALUATING→APPROVED/REJECTED→PROMOTING→ACTIVE
│       │   │
│       │   ├── gates.py
│       │   │     GovernanceGates.check_all() — sequential G1→G7
│       │   │     ApprovalGate, FreqPerfGate, SevPerfGate
│       │   │     MinAucGate (≥0.70), MinR2Gate (≥0.30)
│       │   │     ShadowGate, DriftGate (PSI threshold 0.20)
│       │   │
│       │   ├── atomic.py
│       │   │     AtomicPromotion.execute() — champion_registry.json swap
│       │   │     AtomicPromotion.manual_rollback() — restore previous
│       │   │     reload_predictor() called after swap
│       │   │
│       │   └── shadow.py
│       │         ShadowDeploymentService.record_background()
│       │         Called as FastAPI BackgroundTask from quote_v2.py
│       │
│       ├── db/
│       │   ├── models.py
│       │   │     17 SQLAlchemy ORM table definitions
│       │   │     UUID PKs throughout
│       │   │     JSONB columns: explanation_json, inference_features_json,
│       │   │       model_card, result_json, gates_passed/failed,
│       │   │       evaluation_report, old/new_champion_snapshot
│       │   │
│       │   ├── session.py
│       │   │     Async SQLAlchemy engine (asyncpg)
│       │   │     Sync engine (psycopg2) for LangChain SQL agent
│       │   │     AsyncSessionLocal factory
│       │   │     get_db() dependency injection
│       │   │
│       │   └── checkpointer.py
│       │         setup_checkpointer() — AsyncPostgresCheckpointer
│       │         Used by LangGraph for HITL state persistence
│       │
│       ├── auth/
│       │   └── security.py
│       │         create_access_token() — JWT HS256, 60-min expiry
│       │         decode_token() — verification + HTTP 401
│       │         verify_credentials() — secrets.compare_digest
│       │

── 5. COMPONENT RESPONSIBILITIES ──────────────────────────────────────

  backend/src/api/
    → HTTP surface of the system
    → Routes, schemas, middleware — nothing else
    → All business logic delegated to domain modules

  backend/src/models/
    → ML model wrappers + registry + training scripts
    → No direct DB access (goes through predictor.py)
    → Owns champion_registry.json read path

  backend/src/preprocessing/
    → Full preprocessing pipeline (fit + transform)
    → Shared between training scripts and prediction path
    → Stateful transformers (medians, modes, scalers) persisted in model pkl

  backend/src/engine/
    → Pure business logic: premium calculation + driving score
    → No ML, no DB, no external calls
    → Deterministic, independently testable

  backend/src/agents/
    → AI agent logic only
    → analytics/ owns LangChain SQL agent + session memory
    → underwriting/ owns LangGraph StateGraph + HITL nodes

  backend/src/nlp_assistant/
    → Lower-level SQL agent implementation
    → Security guards (injection, blocked keywords, table whitelist)
    → Used by AnalyticsAgent (analytics/agent.py)

  backend/src/promotions/
    → Model governance pipeline
    → Gates, atomic promotion, shadow deployment
    → Does not touch prediction logic directly

  backend/src/db/
    → All database concerns: ORM models, sessions, checkpointer
    → Async (asyncpg) for routes; sync (psycopg2) for LangChain

  backend/src/auth/
    → Authentication only (JWT create/decode, credential verify)
    → No business logic

  backend/src/core/
    → Application-wide configuration and scheduling
    → Settings singleton (@lru_cache), APScheduler

  frontend/src/pages/
    → Page-level components (route targets)
    → QuoteFormV2Page is primary user journey

  frontend/src/components/
    → Reusable UI components used across multiple pages

  frontend/src/services/
    → HTTP client layer (all API calls go through here)
    → Two Axios instances: V1 and V2

  frontend/src/lib/
    → Pure utility functions (no React hooks, no UI)
    → Client-side derived field computations

  models/ (project root)
    → Artifact store (not source code)
    → Managed by ModelRegistry and AtomicPromotion

  alembic/versions/
    → Authoritative schema history (migrations 001–010)

── 6. INTERACTION BETWEEN COMPONENTS ──────────────────────────────────

  frontend/src/services/api.ts ←→ backend/src/api/routes/*
    → Axios clients call FastAPI route handlers
    → Shared contract via TypeScript types (frontend) + Pydantic (backend)

  backend/src/api/routes/* ←→ backend/src/engine/ + src/models/
    → Routes are thin: validate input → call domain function → persist → return
    → All computation in engine/ and models/

  backend/src/models/predictor.py ←→ backend/src/preprocessing/pipeline.py
    → Predictor owns pipeline instances (DataCleaner, FeatureEngineer, Preprocessor)
    → Pipeline is serialized into model .pkl alongside XGBoost weights

  backend/src/models/model_registry.py ←→ models/metadata/champion_registry.json
    → Registry reads champion paths → loads .pkl files from models/ directory
    → promotions/atomic.py writes to the same file on promotion

  backend/src/agents/analytics/agent.py ←→ backend/src/nlp_assistant/assistant.py
    → Agent calls assistant.query() (delegation pattern)
    → Assistant handles LangChain SQL execution; Agent handles session memory

  backend/src/agents/underwriting/graph.py ←→ backend/src/db/checkpointer.py
    → LangGraph StateGraph compiled with AsyncPostgresCheckpointer
    → State persisted to PostgreSQL between interrupt and resume

  backend/src/promotions/shadow.py ←→ backend/src/api/routes/quote_v2.py
    → shadow.py is called as FastAPI BackgroundTask from quote_v2.py
    → Decoupled: quote response is not delayed by shadow recording

  alembic/versions/ ←→ backend/src/db/models.py
    → Migrations must match ORM model definitions
    → 10 migrations track the schema evolution of 17 ORM tables

── 7. DATA FLOW ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

  Training Time (offline)
    training scripts → preprocessing pipeline fit → XGBoost/CatBoost train
    → .pkl / .cbm files → models/ directory
    → champion_registry.json updated
    → v4_reference_distributions.json updated for PSI baseline

  Request Time (online)
    frontend form → api.ts → FastAPI route → Pydantic schema
    → engine/ (driving score) → models/predictor.py
    → preprocessing/ (pipeline.py) → frequency + severity models
    → engine/calculator.py → premium INR
    → db/ (ORM persist) → models/metadata/ (registry read during predict)
    → HTTP response → frontend pages/ → Recharts visualization

  Governance Time (async)
    quote_v2.py (BackgroundTask) → promotions/shadow.py → db/shadow_predictions
    Admin action → promotions/engine.py → promotions/gates.py
    → promotions/atomic.py → models/ (file swap) → models/predictor.py (reload)
    → db/model_promotions, db/rollback_history, models/metadata/governance_log.json

── 8. SUMMARY ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

  The project directory is a monorepo where backend and frontend are
  cleanly separated. The backend follows domain-driven directory design:
  api/ is the HTTP surface, engine/ is pure business rules, models/ is the
  ML layer, preprocessing/ is the feature engineering pipeline, agents/ is
  AI agent logic, promotions/ is model governance, db/ is all persistence,
  auth/ is authentication, and core/ is configuration. The frontend follows
  a standard React structure with pages/, components/, services/, and lib/
  separated by responsibility. The models/ directory at project root is the
  artifact store managed by ModelRegistry and AtomicPromotion. Alembic
  versions/ is the authoritative schema history with 10 migrations covering
  the full evolution from initial tables through V4 governance columns.
  Every folder has a single clear responsibility, with no cross-cutting
  concerns leaking across domain boundaries.
