INTELLIGENT RISK-BASED MOTOR INSURANCE PREMIUM PRICING SYSTEM

Complete Architecture Documentation — Based Entirely on Actual Source Code

---
---
SECTION 1 — HIGH LEVEL SYSTEM ARCHITECTURE

---
1.1 OVERVIEW

The system is a production-grade, full-stack insurance pricing platform composed of four physical tiers: a React single-page application in the browser, an asynchronous FastAPI service as the application layer, a machine learning and agentic AI intelligence layer, and a PostgreSQL + filesystem persistence layer. Every tier communicates through defined HTTP REST interfaces. No tier has direct knowledge of another tier's internals.

---
1.2 COMPONENTS

COMPONENT LIST — ALL CONFIRMED IN SOURCE CODE

[1]  React 18.2 SPA            — Browser, TypeScript, Vite 5.1
[2]  FastAPI Application       — Python 3.11, async, uvicorn
[3]  JWT Auth Layer            — python-jose, HS256, 60-min expiry
[4]  CORS Middleware           — FastAPI CORSMiddleware
[5]  HTTP Logging Middleware   — Custom per-request logging
[6]  V1 Quote Route            — POST /api/v1/quote
[7]  V2 Quote Route            — POST /api/v2/quote
[8]  Admin Route Group         — All /api/v1/admin/* endpoints
[9]  Governance Route Group    — Training, Approvals, Promotions, Shadow, Drift
[10] Feature Enrichment Svc    — vehicle_specs.json + city_geo_risk.json lookups
[11] Driving Score Engine      — 14-factor rule-based scoring (0-100)
[12] InsurancePreprocessor     — DataCleaner → FeatureEngineer → 33-feature array
[13] FrequencyModel            — XGBClassifier — predicts P(claim)
[14] SeverityModel             — XGBRegressor / CatBoostRegressor — predicts E[amount]
[15] CombinedPredictor         — Orchestrates freq + sev + SHAP
[16] PricingEngine             — Expected Loss × 1.35, 9 business-rule adjustments
[17] SHAP Explainability       — TreeExplainer, top-10 feature contributions
[18] Analytics Agent           — LangChain SQL + Groq Llama 3.3 70B
[19] Underwriting Agent        — LangGraph HITL, 4-node graph, async checkpointer
[20] NLP Assistant             — LangChain SQL agent, InsuranceAssistant class
[21] PostgreSQL 15             — 17 SQLAlchemy ORM tables
[22] Redis                     — Session memory for Analytics Agent (optional)
[23] Filesystem Model Store    — .pkl, .cbm artifacts, champion_registry.json
[24] Champion Registry         — models/metadata/champion_registry.json
[25] Promotion Engine          — Phase5BPromotionEngine, 7 governance gates
[26] Shadow Deployment Svc     — ShadowDeploymentService, background task
[27] Drift Detection Svc       — PSI-based feature distribution monitoring
[28] APScheduler               — Optional weekly retraining cron

---
1.3 STEP-BY-STEP HIGH LEVEL FLOW

STEP 1   Customer opens browser, loads React SPA (Vite dev server / static host)
STEP 2   Customer fills insurance form (V1 12-field or V2 18-field wizard)
STEP 3   React validates input (Zod schema), calls Axios → FastAPI
STEP 4   FastAPI applies CORS check, HTTP logging, JWT check (if admin route)
STEP 5   Pydantic validates and coerces request body
STEP 6   Feature Enrichment Service enriches with lookup tables (V2 only)
STEP 7   Driving Score Engine computes 14-factor safety score
STEP 8   InsurancePreprocessor transforms input → 33-feature numpy array
STEP 9   CombinedPredictor loads champion from registry, calls freq + sev models
STEP 10  FrequencyModel → P(claim) ; SeverityModel → E[amount|claim]
STEP 11  Expected Loss = P(claim) × E[amount]
STEP 12  PricingEngine applies loading factor (×1.35) + 9 sequential adjustments
STEP 13  SHAP TreeExplainer generates feature-level contribution values
STEP 14  Risk classification assigned: low / medium / high
STEP 15  All data written to PostgreSQL (Customer, Vehicle, DrivingProfile, Policy, Prediction)
STEP 16  Shadow prediction recorded as background task (non-blocking)
STEP 17  Response serialised → Pydantic response model → JSON → HTTP 201
STEP 18  React renders premium, risk level, SHAP explanation, enriched features

---

╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╝
                              ↓
╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╗
║         INTELLIGENCE TIER — ML PIPELINE                          ║

║   │  FEATURE ENRICHMENT SERVICE                              │   ║
║   │  vehicle_specs.json → safety_rating, airbags,           │   ║
║   │                       body_style, repair_cost_band       │   ║
║   │  city_geo_risk.json  → flood_risk, theft_risk,          │   ║
║   │                        monsoon, pincode_risk_score       │   ║
║   └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘   ║
║                              ↓                                   ║
║   ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐   ║
║   │  DRIVING SCORE ENGINE (14 factors, base=50, range 0-100) │   ║
║   │  prior claims · mileage · years licensed · age          │   ║
║   │  occupation · marital status · vehicle age · fuel type  │   ║
║   │  vehicle segment · value · usage type · parking         │   ║
║   │  NCB% · city risk category                              │   ║
║   └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘   ║
║                              ↓                                   ║
║   ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐   ║
║   │  PREPROCESSING PIPELINE (InsurancePreprocessor)          │   ║
║   │  Stage 1: DataCleaner (imputation, outlier clipping)     │   ║
║   │  Stage 2: FeatureEngineer (12 derived risk features)     │   ║
║   │  Stage 3: StandardScaler (5) + MinMaxScaler (2)         │   ║
║   │           + OneHotEncoder (13) + LabelEncoder (1)       │   ║
║   │           + Passthrough (12) = 33-feature numpy array   │   ║
║   └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘   ║
║                              ↓                                   ║
║   ┌──────────────────────────┐  ┌──────────────────────────────┐ ║
║   │  FREQUENCY MODEL         │  │  SEVERITY MODEL               │ ║
║   │  XGBClassifier           │  │  XGBRegressor (or CatBoost)   │ ║
║   │  Output: P(claim)        │  │  Output: E[amount | claim]    │ ║
║   │  ∈ [0.0, 1.0]            │  │  INR (expm1 applied)         │ ║
║   └──────────────┬───────────┘  └──────────────┬───────────────┘ ║
║                  └──────────────┬───────────────┘                ║
║                                 ↓                                ║
║   ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐   ║
║   │  EXPECTED LOSS COMPUTATION                                │   ║
║   │  expected_loss = P(claim) × E[amount | claim]            │   ║
║   └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘   ║
║                              ↓                                   ║
║   ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐   ║
║   │  PRICING ENGINE                                           │   ║
║   │  base_premium = expected_loss × 1.35 (loading factor)   │   ║
║   │                                                          │   ║
║   │  BUSINESS RULE ADJUSTMENTS (sequential multiplicative):  │   ║
║   │  age < 25        → ×1.30  (young driver surcharge)      │   ║
║   │  age > 70        → ×1.15  (senior driver surcharge)     │   ║
║   │  driving_score ≥ 85 → ×0.85 (safe driver discount)     │   ║
║   │  driving_score < 50 → ×1.25 (risky driver surcharge)   │   ║
║   │  NCB > 0         → ×(1 - NCB/100) (IRDAI discount)     │   ║
║   │  claims ≥ 3      → ×1.40  (serial claimant surcharge)  │   ║
║   │  mileage > 30000 → ×1.20  (high exposure surcharge)    │   ║
║   │  mileage < 5000  → ×0.92  (low exposure discount)      │   ║
║   │  value > 2000000 → ×1.15  (luxury vehicle surcharge)   │   ║
║   │                                                          │   ║
║   │  final_premium = max(3000, min(150000, adjusted))       │   ║
║   │  risk_level: prob<0.20→low, <0.40→medium, ≥0.40→high   │   ║
║   └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘   ║
║                              ↓                                   ║
║   ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐   ║
║   │  EXPLAINABILITY — SHAP                                   │   ║
║   │  shap.TreeExplainer(model)                               │   ║
║   │  shap_values = explainer.shap_values(X)                 │   ║
║   │  Top 10 features by mean |SHAP| stored in JSONB         │   ║
║   └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘   ║
╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╝
                              ↓
╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╗
║         AGENTIC AI TIER                                          ║
║                                                                  ║
║   ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐   ║
║   │  ANALYTICS AGENT (AnalyticsAgent)                        │   ║
║   │  LangChain SQL agent → Groq Llama 3.3 70B               │   ║
║   │  Redis session memory (TTL 3600s, fallback: in-memory)  │   ║
║   │  Intent classification → anomaly detection →            │   ║
║   │  recommendation generation → answer formatting          │   ║
║   └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘   ║
║                                                                  ║
║   ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐   ║
║   │  UNDERWRITING AGENT (LangGraph HITL)                     │   ║
║   │  4-node graph: fraud_detector → reasoning → decision    │   ║
║   │  → conditional: human_review (interrupt) or END         │   ║
║   │  Groq Llama 3.3 70B for reasoning node                  │   ║
║   │  Async PostgreSQL checkpointer for state persistence     │   ║
║   └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘   ║
╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╝
                              ↓
╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╗
║         PERSISTENCE TIER                                         ║
║                                                                  ║
║   ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐   ║
║   │  PostgreSQL 15 — 17 SQLAlchemy ORM Tables                │   ║
║   │  UUID primary keys throughout                            │   ║
║   │  JSONB for ML outputs, SHAP, model cards, governance     │   ║
║   │  Alembic migrations 001 → 010                           │   ║
║   └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘   ║
║                                                                  ║
║   ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐   ║
║   │  Redis (optional) — Analytics Agent session memory       │   ║
║   │  Keys: agent:analytics:<session_id>                      │   ║
║   │  Fallback: _InMemorySessionMemory (thread-safe dict)     │   ║
║   └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘   ║
║                                                                  ║
║   ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐   ║
║   │  Filesystem Model Store                                  │   ║
║   │  models/saved/           — active champion artifacts     │   ║
║   │  models/metadata/        — champion_registry.json,       │   ║
║   │                            promotion_history.json,       │   ║
║   │                            model_governance_log.json     │   ║
║   │  models/training_runs/   — per-run candidate artifacts   │   ║
║   │  models/challengers/     — pending promotion artifacts   │   ║
║   └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘   ║
╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╝
                              ↓
╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╗
║         RESPONSE — Back to Customer                              ║
║   HTTP 201 Created                                               ║
║   {prediction_id, premium_amount_inr, risk_level,               ║
║    claim_probability, expected_claim_amount_inr,                 ║
║    explanation {base_premium, adjustments[], shap_values},       ║
║    enriched_features, champion_metadata}                         ║
╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╝

---
1.5 COMPONENT RESPONSIBILITIES

COMPONENT                    RESPONSIBILITY
──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
React SPA                    User input, client-side validation,
                             state caching, result rendering

FastAPI Service              Request routing, middleware, auth,
                             orchestration of all backend services

Feature Enrichment           Augments V2 requests with lookup-table
                             data (vehicle specs, city risk indices)

Driving Score Engine         Computes 0-100 safety score from
                             14 deterministic factors

InsurancePreprocessor        Cleans, engineers, and encodes raw
                             input into 33-feature ML array

FrequencyModel               Binary classification: P(claim occurs)

SeverityModel                Regression: E[claim amount | claim]

PricingEngine                Converts expected loss to actuarial
                             premium with business rule adjustments

SHAP Explainability          Decomposes prediction into per-feature
                             contributions stored in JSONB

Analytics Agent              NL → SQL → answer with anomaly
                             detection and recommendations

Underwriting Agent           Fraud detection + LLM reasoning +
                             structured decision + HITL pause

PostgreSQL                   Source of truth for all business data

Redis                        Ephemeral session memory for agents

Filesystem Store             Durable model artifact storage
                             separate from database

---
1.6 SUMMARY

The system implements a three-tier architecture where the React frontend communicates exclusively through a versioned REST API. The backend orchestrates a deterministic ML pipeline (preprocessing → frequency model → severity model → pricing engine → SHAP) and two AI agents (a SQL-based analytics agent and a LangGraph HITL underwriting agent). All ML model management follows a governed lifecycle: training runs produce candidate models that must pass 7 gates before atomic promotion to production, with automatic rollback on failure.

------
SECTION 2 — FRONTEND ARCHITECTURE

---
2.1 OVERVIEW

The frontend is a React 18.2 single-page application written in TypeScript 5.2, bundled by Vite 5.1. All state transitions are managed by React Router v6 for client-side routing and TanStack Query v5 for server state. Forms use React Hook Form with Zod schema validation. Five keys in localStorage provide cross-session persistence for policies, claims, admin token, agent session ID, and feedback state.

---

---
2.3 STEP-BY-STEP FLOW

STEP 1   Browser loads index.html → Vite serves main.tsx
STEP 2   main.tsx wraps App in QueryClientProvider + BrowserRouter
STEP 3   React Router matches URL → renders matched Page inside Layout
STEP 4   Layout renders Navbar + Page content via <Outlet />
STEP 5   AdminProtectedRoute checks localStorage.admin_token
         → absent: redirect to /admin/login
         → present: decode and allow through to AdminPage
STEP 6   Pages call TanStack Query hooks (useQuery / useMutation)
STEP 7   TanStack Query calls Axios function from api/insurance.ts
STEP 8   Axios request interceptor attaches Bearer token if present
STEP 9   Request sent to FastAPI → response received
STEP 10  TanStack Query caches response (staleTime: 30000ms)
STEP 11  React renders updated component tree
STEP 12  localStorage writes on policy purchase / claim submission

---
2.5 DATA FLOW PER PAGE

PAGE               API CALL                      DATA FLOW
──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
HomePage           None                          Static content only

QuotePage(V1)      POST /api/v1/quote            form data
                                                 → apiClient
                                                 → PremiumQuoteResponse
                                                 → QuoteResult renders

QuotePageV2        POST /api/v2/quote/preview    form data (18 fields)
                   POST /api/v2/quote            → enriched_features
                                                 → QuoteResponseV2
                                                 → QuoteResultV2 renders

PolicyPage         GET /api/v1/policies/{id}     policy_id from URL
                                                 → policy + prediction
                                                 + SHAP explanation_json

MyPoliciesPage     None (localStorage read)      insureai_policies
                                                 → renders list

ClaimsPage         POST /api/v1/claims           policy_id + amount
                   GET /api/v1/claims/{id}       → claim object
                                                 → status display

AdminLoginPage     POST /api/v1/auth/login       {username, password}
                                                 → access_token
                                                 → write localStorage
                                                 → redirect /admin

AdminPage          Multiple admin endpoints      See TAB descriptions
                   via TanStack Query hooks      above

---
2.6 SUMMARY

The frontend is structured as a two-track application: a customer-facing track (quote wizard, policies, claims) and an admin track (protected, 11-panel dashboard). The V2 quote wizard introduces a 5-step progressive form with live preview (enrichment-only API call before full ML). TanStack Query handles all server state with selective polling (5-second for HITL status, 30-second for review queue). Client-side calculations (NCB, vehicle value estimate) reduce unnecessary preview API calls during wizard navigation.

------
SECTION 3 — BACKEND ARCHITECTURE

---
3.1 OVERVIEW

The backend is a FastAPI application (async, Python 3.11) defined in src/api/main.py. It uses a lifespan context manager for startup/shutdown, registers 13+ routers, applies two middleware layers, and orchestrates the ML pipeline and AI agents. All route handlers use SQLAlchemy async sessions and Pydantic models for serialisation.

---
3.2 STEP-BY-STEP REQUEST FLOW

STEP 1   HTTP request arrives at uvicorn → FastAPI
STEP 2   CORSMiddleware validates Origin header
STEP 3   HTTP logging middleware records request start timestamp
STEP 4   FastAPI router matches request to handler function
STEP 5   Handler dependency (Depends) resolved:
         get_db() → async SQLAlchemy session
         get_current_admin() → decode JWT, raise 401 if invalid
STEP 6   Pydantic model validates request body (type coercion, constraints)
STEP 7   Handler calls service functions (enrichment, ML, pricing)
STEP 8   ML pipeline executes (see Section 5)
STEP 9   SQLAlchemy adds records to session → await db.flush()
STEP 10  Background tasks registered (shadow deployment)
STEP 11  Response model serialised to JSON
STEP 12  HTTP logging middleware records response status + duration
STEP 13  Response returned to client

║                                                                  ║
║  security.py                                                     ║
║  ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐  ║
║  │  verify_credentials(username, password)                   │  ║
║  │    secrets.compare_digest(username, settings.ADMIN_USERNAME│  ║
║  │    secrets.compare_digest(password, settings.ADMIN_PASSWORD│  ║
║  │    Returns: True / False (timing-safe)                    │  ║
║  │                                                           │  ║
║  │  create_access_token(username)                            │  ║
║  │    payload = {sub: username, role: "admin", iat, exp}     │  ║
║  │    jwt.encode(payload, JWT_SECRET_KEY, algorithm="HS256")  │  ║
║  │    expiry = now + JWT_EXPIRE_MINUTES (default 60)         │  ║
║  │                                                           │  ║
║  │  decode_token(token)                                      │  ║
║  │    jwt.decode(token, JWT_SECRET_KEY, algorithms=["HS256"]) │  ║
║  │    Raises HTTP 401 on JWTError                            │  ║
║  └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘  ║
║                                                                  ║

║  │    → FastAPI dependency injected into protected routes    │  ║

║  ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐  ║
║  │  enrich_v2_request(request: CustomerQuoteRequestV2)        │  ║
║  │  → loads vehicle_specs.json via lookup_loader              │  ║
║  │    match on (vehicle_make, vehicle_model)                  │  ║
║  │    returns vehicle_safety_rating, airbags_count,           │  ║
║  │            vehicle_body_style, repair_cost_band            │  ║

║  │  → loads city_geo_risk.json via lookup_loader              │  ║
║  │    match on city name                                      │  ║
║  │    returns region_risk_category, flood_risk_index,         │  ║
║  │            theft_risk_index, monsoon_exposure_index,       │  ║
║  │            pincode_risk_score                              │  ║
║  │  → calls calculate_driving_score() (14 factors)            │  ║
║  │  → derives no_claim_bonus_pct (IRDAI schedule)            │  ║
║  │  → sets policy_inception_month = current month             │  ║
║  │  → builds inference_payload: aliased for V1 preprocessor  │  ║
║  │    (vehicle_make/model → car_brand/car_model)              │  ║
║  │  Returns: {enrichment_meta, inference_payload}             │  ║

║                                                                  ║
║  DRIFT DETECTION — services/drift_detection.py                  ║
║  ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐  ║
║  │  PSI (Population Stability Index) computation              │  ║
║  │  Reference: models/metadata/v4_reference_distributions.json│  ║
║  │  Current: recent inference feature distributions from DB   │  ║
║  │  PSI > 0.2 → "High Drift" alert                           │  ║
║  │  GET /api/v1/drift/report → DriftReport (PSI per feature)  │  ║
║  └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘  ║
║                                                                  ║
║  SHADOW DEPLOYMENT — services/shadow_deployment.py              ║

║  │  → inserts shadow_predictions row                          │  ║
║  │  → comparison_result computed if challenger present        │  ║
║  │  Runs as BackgroundTask — does NOT block response          │  ║

                              ↓
            [ML PIPELINE — see Section 5]
                              ↓
╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╗
║  DATABASE WRITE — SQLAlchemy async                               ║
║                                                                  ║
║  For V1/V2 Quote:                                                ║
║  ① db.add(Customer) → await db.flush() → customer_id           ║
║  ② db.add(Vehicle)  → stores all V4 enriched columns           ║
║  ③ db.add(DrivingProfile) → stores geo-risk V4 columns         ║
║  ④ db.add(Policy) → premium_amount_inr, risk_level,            ║
║                     model_version, is_active=False             ║
║  ⑤ await db.flush() → policy_id generated                      ║
║  ⑥ db.add(Prediction) → claim_probability,                     ║
║                          expected_claim_amount_inr,             ║
║                          final_premium_inr,                     ║
║                          explanation_json (SHAP + adjustments), ║
║                          inference_features_json (payload snap) ║
║  ⑦ await db.flush() → prediction_id generated                  ║
║  ⑧ (implicit commit via session context)                       ║
╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╝
                              ↓
╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╗
║  API RESPONSE                                                    ║
║                                                                  ║
║  V1: PremiumQuoteResponse                                        ║
║    prediction_id, premium_amount_inr, risk_level,               ║
║    claim_probability, expected_claim_amount_inr,                 ║
║    explanation {base_premium, expected_loss, adjustments[],     ║
║                 final_premium, risk_level, claim_probability,    ║
║                 summary},                                        ║
║    model_version, created_at, driving_score_info               ║
║                                                                  ║
║  V2: QuoteResponseV2 (extends V1 response + adds)               ║
║    enriched_features {                                           ║
║      driving_score {score, factors[]},                           ║
║      vehicle {safety_rating, airbags, body_style,               ║
║               repair_cost_band, lookup_hit},                     ║
║      city {region_risk, flood_risk, theft_risk,                  ║
║            monsoon, pincode_risk, lookup_hit},                   ║
║      policy_inception_month, no_claim_bonus_pct                  ║
║    },                                                            ║
║    champion_metadata {                                           ║
║      frequency_model, severity_model,                            ║
║      model_version, v4_ready                                     ║
║    }                                                             ║
║  HTTP 201 Created                                                ║
╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╝

---
3.4 SUMMARY

The backend applies a clean layered pattern: middleware → auth → routing → validation → services → ML → DB → response. Optional agent routers are registered in try/except blocks so startup never fails due to missing AI dependencies. The lifespan event loads ML models and the LangGraph graph into global singletons so inference has zero cold-start per request. SQLAlchemy async sessions are yielded per-request and committed automatically. Background tasks (shadow deployment) are registered but execute after the HTTP response is sent.

------
SECTION 4 — DATABASE ARCHITECTURE

---
4.1 OVERVIEW

PostgreSQL 15 hosts 17 SQLAlchemy ORM tables. All primary keys are UUID type. JSONB columns store ML predictions, SHAP explanations, model cards, governance logs, and agent state snapshots. Schema history is managed via 10 Alembic migrations (001 initial schema → 010 model promotions). Two session types exist: async (asyncpg driver, used by all FastAPI routes) and sync (psycopg2, used by LangChain SQL agent).

---

---
4.3 ENTITY RELATIONSHIP FLOW

DATA FLOW THROUGH DOMAIN TABLES

Customer submits quote
         ↓
customers row created (age, gender, city + V4 demographics)
         │
         ├──→ vehicles row created (make, model + V4 enriched specs)
         │
         ├──→ driving_profiles row created (score, mileage + V4 geo-risk)
         │
         └──→ policies row created (premium, risk_level, model_version)
                          │
                          └──→ predictions row created
                                  (claim_probability,
                                   expected_claim_amount,
                                   final_premium_inr,
                                   explanation_json [SHAP],
                                   inference_features_json [raw])
                                  │
                                  ├──→ claims rows (when customer files)
                                  │
                                  └──→ model_feedback rows (actual outcomes)

Training pipeline runs
         ↓
training_runs row created (status: pending → running → completed)
         │
         └──→ candidate_models rows (one per algorithm × model_type)
                          │
                          └──→ approval_requests row (model_card JSONB)
                                          │
                                          └──→ approval_audit_logs rows
                                          │
                                          ↓ (if approved)
                                  model_promotions row
                                  (EVALUATING → APPROVED → ACTIVE)
                                          │
                                          └──→ rollback_history
                                               (if promotion fails or
                                                manual rollback triggered)

Each V2 quote also creates
         ↓
shadow_predictions row (champion prediction snapshot)
(challenger prediction added later when challenger is available)

AI agents execute
         ↓
agent_runs row (status tracks HITL lifecycle)
         │
         └──→ agent_tool_calls rows (each tool invocation logged)
         │
         └──→ model_feedback row (human + agent decision outcome)

---
4.4 ALEMBIC MIGRATION HISTORY

001_initial_schema.py
    Creates: customers, vehicles, driving_profiles, policies,
             predictions, claims, model_versions

002_agent_tables.py
    Creates: agent_runs, agent_tool_calls, model_feedback

003_hitl_columns.py
    Adds to agent_runs: review_outcome, reviewed_at, reviewer_note,
                        result_json, prediction_id

004_retraining_enrichment.py
    Creates: approval_requests, approval_audit_logs, training_runs

005_ev_engine_cc.py
    Adds to vehicles: fuel_type (EV support)

006_v4_feature_columns.py
    Adds V4 columns to customers, vehicles, driving_profiles

007_approval_workflow.py
    Creates: candidate_models
    Extends: approval_requests

008_shadow_deployment.py
    Creates: shadow_predictions

009_training_runs.py
    Extends: training_runs with additional metrics columns

010_model_promotions.py
    Creates: model_promotions, rollback_history

---
4.5 SUMMARY

The database is organised into three logical domains: the Customer Domain (core business data: customers, vehicles, profiles, policies, predictions, claims, feedback), the Agent Domain (AI execution logs: agent_runs, agent_tool_calls), and the Governance Domain (ML lifecycle: training_runs, candidate_models, approval_requests, approval_audit_logs, model_promotions, rollback_history, shadow_predictions, model_versions). JSONB columns are used strategically for schema-flexible data (SHAP values, model cards, state snapshots) while structured columns with CHECK constraints enforce business rules at the database layer.

------
SECTION 5 — MACHINE LEARNING ARCHITECTURE

---
5.1 OVERVIEW

The ML pipeline implements an actuarial two-model compound prediction architecture: a frequency model predicts the probability of a claim occurring, and a severity model predicts the expected claim amount given that a claim occurs. Their product yields Expected Loss, which the pricing engine converts to a final premium using a loading factor and business rules. SHAP provides per-prediction explainability. Three algorithms (XGBoost, LightGBM, CatBoost) are available; the active champion is determined by the champion registry.

---
5.2 DETAILED HIERARCHICAL FLOWCHART

╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╗
║  INPUT — Raw request dict (from V1 or V2 quote endpoint)         ║
║                                                                  ║
║  Core fields (always present):                                   ║
║    age, gender, city, annual_mileage_km, previous_claims_count, ║
║    years_licensed, driving_score, vehicle_value_inr,            ║
║    vehicle_age_years, vehicle_brand, vehicle_segment,           ║
║    fuel_type, annual_income_inr                                  ║
╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╝
                              ↓
╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╗
║  COMBINED PREDICTOR — src/models/combined_predictor.py           ║
║                                                                  ║
║  LOADING PRIORITY (startup, then hot-reload after promotion):    ║
║  ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐  ║
║  │  ATTEMPT 1 — Champion Registry                            │  ║
║  │    Read champion_registry.json                            │  ║
║  │    Check frequency["deployment_status"] == "active"       │  ║
║  │    Check severity["deployment_status"] == "active"        │  ║
║  │    Load freq champion .pkl + paired preprocessor.pkl      │  ║
║  │    Load sev champion .pkl or .cbm (CatBoost)              │  ║
║  │    Sets _loaded_from_registry = True                      │  ║
║  └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘  ║
║  ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐  ║
║  │  ATTEMPT 2 — V1 Legacy Fallback                           │  ║
║  │    models/saved/preprocessor.pkl                          │  ║
║  │    models/saved/frequency_v1.0.0.pkl                      │  ║
║  │    models/saved/severity_v1.0.0.pkl                       │  ║
║  │    Sets _loaded_from_registry = False                     │  ║
║  └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘  ║
║                                                                  ║
║  HOT-RELOAD — after promotion (no restart needed):              ║
║    reload_predictor() atomically replaces global _predictor     ║
╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╝
                              ↓
╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╗
║  PREPROCESSING PIPELINE — src/preprocessing/pipeline.py          ║
║  InsurancePreprocessor (sklearn BaseEstimator + TransformerMixin)║
╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╝
                              ↓
╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╗
║  STAGE 1 — DataCleaner                                           ║
║                                                                  ║
║  fit() phase (training only):                                    ║
║    Computes and stores medians for 8 numeric columns             ║
║    Computes and stores modes for 4 categorical columns           ║
║                                                                  ║
║  transform() phase (training + inference):                       ║
║  ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐  ║
║  │  Drop duplicates on customer_id (if present)              │  ║
║  │  Fill numeric NAs with fitted medians:                    │  ║
║  │    age, annual_mileage_km, driving_score,                 │  ║
║  │    vehicle_value_inr, vehicle_age_years, years_licensed,  │  ║
║  │    previous_claims_count, annual_income_inr               │  ║
║  │  Fill categorical NAs with fitted modes:                  │  ║
║  │    city, gender, vehicle_segment, fuel_type               │  ║
║  │  Clip outliers:                                           │  ║
║  │    annual_mileage_km → [0, 200000]                        │  ║
║  │    age → [18, 85]                                         │  ║
║  │    driving_score → [0, 100]                               │  ║
║  │    annual_income_inr → [0, 50000000]                      │  ║
║  └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘  ║
╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╝
                              ↓
╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╗
║  STAGE 2 — FeatureEngineer                                       ║
║                                                                  ║
║  Column aliasing (backward compat):                              ║
║    vehicle_brand → car_brand (if car_brand absent)               ║
║    previous_claims → previous_claims_count (if absent)          ║
║                                                                  ║
║  Normalisation:                                                  ║
║    gender → lowercase                                            ║
║    vehicle_segment → canonical (Hatchback/Sedan/SUV/Luxury)     ║
║    fuel_type → canonical (Petrol/Diesel/CNG/EV)                 ║
║    annual_income_inr → default = 2596312.0 (dataset mean)       ║
║                                                                  ║
║  DERIVED FEATURES — 12 new columns added:                        ║
║  ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐  ║
║  │  1. risk_age_ratio                                        │  ║
║  │     = previous_claims / max(years_licensed, 1)           │  ║
║  │     Meaning: Annualised claim rate                        │  ║
║  │                                                           │  ║
║  │  2. vehicle_depreciation_factor                           │  ║
║  │     = 1 / (1 + vehicle_age_years × 0.15)                │  ║
║  │     Meaning: Current market value proxy                  │  ║
║  │                                                           │  ║
║  │  3. normalized_mileage                                    │  ║
║  │     = annual_mileage_km / 15000                          │  ║
║  │     Meaning: Exposure normalisation against fleet avg    │  ║
║  │                                                           │  ║
║  │  4. high_value_vehicle                                    │  ║
║  │     = 1 if vehicle_value_inr > 1500000, else 0           │  ║
║  │     Meaning: Binary luxury flag                          │  ║
║  │                                                           │  ║
║  │  5. city_tier                                             │  ║
║  │     = CITY_TIER_MAP.get(city.lower(), 3)                 │  ║
║  │     Tier 1: Mumbai, Delhi, Bangalore, Hyderabad,         │  ║
║  │             Chennai, Kolkata, Pune, Ahmedabad            │  ║
║  │     Tier 2: 12 additional cities                         │  ║
║  │     Tier 3: All others (default)                         │  ║
║  │     Meaning: Urban density risk                          │  ║
║  │                                                           │  ║
║  │  6. driving_risk_score                                    │  ║
║  │     = (100 - driving_score)/100 × (1 + claims × 0.2)    │  ║
║  │     Meaning: Combined behavioural risk index             │  ║
║  │                                                           │  ║
║  │  7. vehicle_value_per_age                                 │  ║
║  │     = vehicle_value_inr / (1 + vehicle_age_years)        │  ║
║  │     Meaning: Repair cost proxy                           │  ║
║  │                                                           │  ║
║  │  8. income_to_vehicle_ratio                               │  ║
║  │     = annual_income_inr / max(vehicle_value_inr, 1)      │  ║
║  │     Meaning: Moral hazard / underinsurance indicator     │  ║
║  │                                                           │  ║
║  │  9. exposure_risk_index                                   │  ║
║  │     = normalized_mileage × (1 + claims × 0.2)           │  ║
║  │     Meaning: Exposure weighted by claim history          │  ║
║  │                                                           │  ║
║  │  10. driver_experience_ratio                              │  ║
║  │      = years_licensed / max(age - 18, 1), capped at 1.0 │  ║
║  │      Meaning: Proportion of adult life spent driving     │  ║
║  │                                                           │  ║
║  │  11. claim_frequency_rate                                 │  ║
║  │      = previous_claims / max(years_licensed, 1)          │  ║
║  │      Meaning: Serial claimant signal                     │  ║
║  │                                                           │  ║
║  │  12. vehicle_premium_stress                               │  ║
║  │      = vehicle_value / max(annual_income, 1), cap 10.0   │  ║
║  │      Meaning: Underinsurance / financial stress proxy    │  ║
║  │                                                           │  ║
║  │  car_brand_encoded:                                       │  ║
║  │      = brand if brand in TOP_BRANDS else "other"         │  ║
║  │      TOP_BRANDS: maruti, hyundai, tata, mahindra, honda, │  ║
║  │      toyota, ford, volkswagen, kia, renault, skoda, mg,  │  ║
║  │      nissan, jeep, bmw                                    │  ║
║  └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘  ║
╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╝
                              ↓
╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╗
║  STAGE 3 — ENCODING AND SCALING → 33-FEATURE NUMPY ARRAY         ║
║                                                                  ║
║  GROUP A — StandardScaler (5 features)                           ║
║  ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐  ║
║  │  age, annual_mileage_km, vehicle_value_inr,               │  ║
║  │  driving_score, annual_income_inr                         │  ║
║  │  z-score normalisation: (x - mean) / std                  │  ║
║  └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘  ║
║                                                                  ║
║  GROUP B — MinMaxScaler (2 features)                             ║
║  ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐  ║
║  │  vehicle_age_years, years_licensed                        │  ║
║  │  [0, 1] range normalisation                               │  ║
║  └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘  ║
║                                                                  ║
║  GROUP C — OneHotEncoder (13 categories)                         ║
║  ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐  ║
║  │  gender          → 2 cols (male, female)                  │  ║
║  │  city_tier       → 3 cols (tier_1, tier_2, tier_3)        │  ║
║  │  vehicle_segment → 4 cols (Hatchback, Sedan, SUV, Luxury) │  ║
║  │  fuel_type       → 4 cols (Petrol, Diesel, CNG, EV)       │  ║
║  │  handle_unknown = "ignore"                                │  ║
║  └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘  ║
║                                                                  ║
║  GROUP D — LabelEncoder (1 feature)                              ║
║  ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐  ║
║  │  car_brand_encoded                                        │  ║
║  │  Unknown brands mapped to "other" before encoding         │  ║
║  └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘  ║
║                                                                  ║
║  GROUP E — Passthrough (12 features)                             ║
║  ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐  ║
║  │  All 12 derived features from Stage 2                    │  ║
║  │  (no scaling applied)                                     │  ║
║  └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘  ║
║                                                                  ║
║  np.hstack([standard_scaled, minmax_scaled, ohe_encoded,        ║
║             brand_encoded, passthrough])                         ║
║  → shape: (n_samples, 33)                                        ║
╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╝
                              ↓
       ┌──────────────────────┴──────────────────────┐
       ↓                                             ↓
╔══════════════════════════╗         ╔═══════════════════════════╗
║  FREQUENCY MODEL          ║         ║  SEVERITY MODEL            ║
║  src/models/frequency_    ║         ║  src/models/severity_      ║
║  model.py                 ║         ║  model.py                  ║
║                           ║         ║                            ║
║  FrequencyModel wrapper   ║         ║  SeverityModel wrapper     ║
║                           ║         ║  (or CatBoostSeverity-     ║
║  ALGORITHM: XGBClassifier ║         ║  Wrapper for CatBoost)     ║
║  (XGBoost default)        ║         ║                            ║
║  GridSearchCV (3-fold):   ║         ║  ALGORITHM: XGBRegressor   ║
║    n_estimators: 100/200  ║         ║  (or CatBoostRegressor)    ║
║    max_depth: 4/6         ║         ║  GridSearchCV (3-fold):    ║
║    learning_rate:0.05/0.1 ║         ║    n_estimators: 100/200   ║
║  scale_pos_weight =       ║         ║    max_depth: 4/6          ║
║    neg_count / pos_count  ║         ║    learning_rate:0.05/0.1  ║
║  (handles class imbalance)║         ║                            ║
║                           ║         ║  TARGET ENCODING:          ║
║  TRAINING TARGET:         ║         ║    y_train = log1p(amount) ║
║    claim_occurred (0/1)   ║         ║    (log transform applied  ║
║                           ║         ║     to reduce skew)        ║
║  EVALUATION METRICS:      ║         ║                            ║
║    AUC-ROC                ║         ║  PREDICTION:               ║
║    Precision              ║         ║    log_pred = model.predict ║
║    Recall                 ║         ║    return np.expm1(log_pred)║
║    F1                     ║         ║    (inverse log transform)  ║
║    Brier Score            ║         ║                            ║
║                           ║         ║  EVALUATION METRICS:       ║
║  INFERENCE:               ║         ║    RMSE, MAE, R², MAPE     ║
║    model.predict_proba(X) ║         ║                            ║
║    → [:, 1]               ║         ║  INFERENCE:                ║
║    = P(claim) ∈ [0, 1]    ║         ║    model.predict(X)        ║
║                           ║         ║    → expm1 applied         ║
╚══════════════════════════╝         ║    = E[claim amount] INR   ║
                                     ╚═══════════════════════════╝
       ↓ P(claim)                              ↓ E[amount]
       └──────────────────┬────────────────────┘
                          ↓
╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╗
║  EXPECTED LOSS COMPUTATION                                       ║
║                                                                  ║
║  expected_loss = claim_probability × expected_claim_amount_inr   ║
║                                                                  ║
║  Example: P=0.15, E=₹80,000 → expected_loss = ₹12,000           ║
╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╝
                              ↓
╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╗
║  PRICING ENGINE — src/engine/pricing_engine.py                   ║
║                                                                  ║
║  CONSTANTS:                                                      ║
║    LOADING_FACTOR = 1.35                                         ║
║    MIN_PREMIUM_INR = 3000                                        ║
║    MAX_PREMIUM_INR = 150000                                      ║
║                                                                  ║
║  base_premium = expected_loss × 1.35                             ║
║                                                                  ║
║  SEQUENTIAL BUSINESS RULE ADJUSTMENTS:                           ║
║  running_premium = base_premium                                  ║
║  ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐  ║
║  │  Rule 1: Age surcharge                                    │  ║
║  │    if age < 25:   running × 1.30 (young driver)          │  ║
║  │    if age > 70:   running × 1.15 (senior driver)         │  ║
║  │                                                           │  ║
║  │  Rule 2: Driving score adjustment                         │  ║
║  │    if score ≥ 85: running × 0.85 (safe driver reward)    │  ║
║  │    if score < 50: running × 1.25 (risky driver penalty)  │  ║
║  │                                                           │  ║
║  │  Rule 3: No-Claim Bonus (IRDAI mandated)                  │  ║
║  │    if ncb_pct > 0: running × (1 - ncb_pct / 100)        │  ║
║  │    0% NCB → no change                                     │  ║
║  │    20% NCB → × 0.80                                       │  ║
║  │    50% NCB → × 0.50                                       │  ║
║  │                                                           │  ║
║  │  Rule 4: Serial claimant surcharge                        │  ║
║  │    if previous_claims ≥ 3: running × 1.40                │  ║
║  │                                                           │  ║
║  │  Rule 5: Mileage adjustment                               │  ║
║  │    if mileage > 30000: running × 1.20 (high exposure)    │  ║
║  │    if mileage < 5000:  running × 0.92 (low exposure)     │  ║
║  │                                                           │  ║
║  │  Rule 6: Luxury vehicle surcharge                         │  ║
║  │    if value > 2000000: running × 1.15                    │  ║
║  └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘  ║
║                                                                  ║
║  FLOOR/CEILING CLAMP:                                            ║
║    final_premium = max(3000, min(150000, running_premium))       ║
║                                                                  ║
║  RISK CLASSIFICATION:                                            ║
║    claim_probability < 0.20  → risk_level = "low"               ║
║    claim_probability < 0.40  → risk_level = "medium"            ║
║    claim_probability ≥ 0.40  → risk_level = "high"              ║
╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╝
                              ↓
╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╗
║  EXPLAINABILITY — SHAP                                           ║
║  Both FrequencyModel and SeverityModel implement:                ║
║                                                                  ║
║  get_shap_values(X: np.ndarray)                                  ║
║    explainer = shap.TreeExplainer(self.model)                   ║
║    shap_values = explainer.shap_values(X)                       ║
║    returns (shap_values array, explainer object)                 ║
║                                                                  ║
║  get_top_features(shap_values, feature_names, n=10)              ║
║    mean_shap = np.abs(shap_values).mean(axis=0)                 ║
║    top_idx = np.argsort(mean_shap)[-n:][::-1]                   ║
║    returns dict: {feature_name: mean_abs_shap_value}            ║
║                                                                  ║
║  STORED IN: predictions.explanation_json (JSONB)                ║
║  RETURNED IN: every API response under explanation.shap_values  ║
╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╝
                              ↓
╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╗
║  FINAL PREDICTION OBJECT                                         ║
║                                                                  ║
║  {                                                               ║
║    "claim_probability": 0.15,                                    ║
║    "expected_claim_amount_inr": 80000.0,                         ║
║    "expected_loss": 12000.0,                                     ║
║    "base_premium": 16200.0,                                      ║
║    "final_premium": 18630.0,                                     ║
║    "risk_level": "low",                                          ║
║    "adjustments": [                                              ║
║      {"reason": "Safe driver discount", "factor": 0.85},         ║
║      {"reason": "NCB 20%", "factor": 0.80}                       ║
║    ],                                                            ║
║    "shap_values": {                                              ║
║      "driving_score": -0.034,                                    ║
║      "previous_claims_count": 0.021,                             ║
║      "city_tier": 0.018,                                         ║
║      ...top 10 features                                          ║
║    }                                                             ║
║  }                                                               ║
╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╝

╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╗
║  MULTI-ALGORITHM TRAINING ENGINE                                  ║
║  src/training/multi_algorithm_engine.py                          ║
║                                                                  ║
║  Trains 3 algorithms × 2 model types in parallel:               ║
║                                                                  ║
║  FREQUENCY (classification):                                     ║
║    XGBoost  → trainers/xgboost_trainer.py  → AUC-ROC scoring    ║
║    LightGBM → trainers/lightgbm_trainer.py → AUC-ROC scoring    ║
║    CatBoost → trainers/catboost_trainer.py → AUC-ROC scoring    ║
║                                                                  ║
║  SEVERITY (regression):                                          ║
║    XGBoost  → log1p target, neg_RMSE scoring                    ║
║    LightGBM → log1p target, neg_RMSE scoring                    ║
║    CatBoost → raw INR target (CatBoost handles internally)      ║
║                                                                  ║
║  OUTPUTS per run (models/training_runs/<run_tag>/):             ║
║    frequency/xgboost/metadata.json                              ║
║    frequency/xgboost/feature_importance.json                    ║
║    frequency/lightgbm/...                                       ║
║    frequency/catboost/...                                       ║
║    frequency/shap_importance.json                               ║
║    severity/xgboost/...  (same structure)                       ║
║    severity/lightgbm/...                                        ║
║    severity/catboost/...                                        ║
║    run_metadata.json                                            ║
╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╝

---
5.3 SUMMARY

The ML pipeline is a compound actuarial model where frequency (P(claim)) and severity (E[amount|claim]) are predicted independently, then multiplied to yield Expected Loss. The loading factor (1.35) converts expected loss to base premium, then sequential business rules (6 adjustments) produce the final premium. All predictions have mandatory SHAP explainability with top-10 feature contributions stored in JSONB. Three algorithms are available; the active champion is loaded by the Champion Registry at startup with hot-reload capability.

------
SECTION 6 — MODEL GOVERNANCE ARCHITECTURE

---
6.1 OVERVIEW

Model governance is implemented through a complete lifecycle pipeline: training → candidate evaluation → manual approval → governance gate evaluation → atomic promotion → production monitoring → rollback. Every step is persisted to the database (training_runs, candidate_models, approval_requests, model_promotions, rollback_history) and to the filesystem (champion_registry.json, promotion_history.json, model_governance_log.json).

---
6.2 DETAILED HIERARCHICAL FLOWCHART

╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╗
║  PHASE 1 — TRAINING                                              ║
║  src/training/multi_algorithm_engine.py                          ║
╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╝
                              ↓
TRIGGER: POST /api/v1/training/start  (admin)
         OR  APScheduler weekly cron (if ENABLE_SCHEDULED_RETRAINING=True)
         OR  POST /api/v1/retraining/trigger
                              ↓
training_runs row created
  status: pending → running
  run_tag: unique identifier (e.g. run_20260627_123459_e16f2e60)
                              ↓
multi_algorithm_engine trains:
  XGBoost freq + XGBoost sev
  LightGBM freq + LightGBM sev
  CatBoost freq + CatBoost sev
  (3-fold GridSearchCV each, parallel where possible)
                              ↓
Artifacts saved to:
  models/training_runs/<run_tag>/frequency/{xgboost,lightgbm,catboost}/
  models/training_runs/<run_tag>/severity/{xgboost,lightgbm,catboost}/
  models/training_runs/<run_tag>/run_metadata.json
                              ↓
6 candidate_models rows created in DB
  (one per algorithm × model_type)
                              ↓
training_runs.status: running → completed
  training_runs.summary JSONB updated with metrics comparison

╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╗
║  PHASE 2 — MODEL CARD GENERATION                                 ║
║  src/services/model_card.py                                      ║
╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╝
                              ↓
For selected candidate (best AUC/R² algorithm):
  Model card generated containing:
    accuracy_metrics (AUC-ROC, Precision, Recall, F1, Brier)
    feature_importance (SHAP top-10)
    training_data_summary (rows, splits, features)
    algorithm + hyperparameters
    evaluation date
                              ↓
approval_requests row created:
  status: pending
  model_card: JSONB
  submitted_by: "system" or admin username
  recommendation: "Approve" / "Reject" (system suggestion)

╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╗

║  src/api/routes/approvals.py                                     ║
╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╝
                              ↓
Admin reviews in AdminDashboard → Approval Panel:
  Reads: model_card JSONB (metrics, feature importance, training summary)
  Decision: Approve / Reject
                              ↓
POST /api/v1/approvals/{id}/approve  (or /reject)
  approval_requests.status → "approved" or "rejected"
  approval_requests.reviewed_by = admin username
  approval_requests.reviewed_at = timestamp
                              ↓
approval_audit_logs row appended:
  event_type: "approved" or "rejected"
  actor: admin username
  note: reviewer_note
  event_data: JSONB snapshot
